V_{i}^{*,\pi_{-i}}(s)=\min_{\pi_{i}}\max_{p_{i}}\pi_{i}(\cdot|s)^{T}Q_{i}^{*,\pi_{-i}}(s,\cdot)p_{i}(\cdot|s)-\frac{1}{\tau_{i}}D_{i}(p_{i},\pi_{-i};s)+\epsilon_{i}\nu_{i}(\pi_{i};s),

(38)

and let
\pi_{i}^{*},p_{i}^{*}
be the corresponding minimax solution, we have that
V_{i}^{\pi_{i}^{*},\pi_{-i},p_{i}^{*}}=V_{i}^{*,\pi_{-i}}
because:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\pi_{i}^{*},\pi_{-i},p_{i}}Q_{i}^{*,\pi_{-i}}(s,\mathbf{a})

(39)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-r_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})+\gamma\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot|s,\mathbf{a})}[(\pi_{i}^{*})^{T}(\cdot|s^{\prime})Q^{*,\pi_{-i}}_{i}(s^{\prime},\cdot)p^{*}_{i}(\cdot|s^{\prime})-\frac{1}{\tau_{i}}D_{i}(p_{i}^{*},\pi_{-i};s^{\prime})+\epsilon_{i}\nu_{i}(\pi^{*}_{i};s^{\prime})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-r_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})+\gamma\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot|s,\mathbf{a})}[\min_{\pi_{i}}\max_{p_{i}}\pi_{i}^{T}(\cdot|s^{\prime})Q^{*,\pi_{-i}}_{i}(s^{\prime},\cdot)p_{i}(\cdot|s^{\prime})-\frac{1}{\tau_{i}}D_{i}(p_{i},\pi_{-i};s^{\prime})+\epsilon_{i}\nu_{i}(\pi_{i};s^{\prime})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\pi_{-i}}Q_{i}^{*,\pi_{-i}}(s,\mathbf{a})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle Q_{i}^{*,\pi_{-i}}(s,\mathbf{a})

so
Q_{i}^{*,\pi_{-i}}=Q_{i}^{\pi_{i}^{*},\pi_{-i},p_{i}^{*}}
because the unique fixed point of
\mathcal{T}_{\pi_{i}^{*},\pi_{-i},p_{i}}
is
Q_{i}^{\pi_{i}^{*},\pi_{-i},p_{i}^{*}}
(by a similar contraction statement on
\mathcal{T}_{\pi_{i}^{*},\pi_{-i},p_{i}}
).

Similarly, let
\pi_{i}^{\dagger},p_{i}^{\dagger}
be the maximin solution to
\max_{p_{i}}\min_{\pi_{i}}V_{i}^{\pi_{i},\pi_{-i},p_{i}}(s)
, by similar rationale we know that
Q^{\pi_{i}^{\dagger},\pi_{-i},p_{i}^{\dagger}}
is a unique fixed point of the maximin Bellman operator
\mathcal{T}_{\pi_{i}^{*},\pi_{-i},p_{i}}^{\prime}
defined by:

\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{\pi_{-i}}Q_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})=-r_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})+\gamma\max_{p_{i}}\min_{\pi_{i}}\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot|s,\mathbf{a})}[\pi_{i}^{T}(\cdot|s^{\prime})Q_{i}(s^{\prime},\cdot)p_{i}(\cdot|s^{\prime})-\frac{1}{\tau_{i}}D_{i}(p_{i},\pi_{-i};s^{\prime})+\epsilon_{i}\nu_{i}(\pi_{i};s^{\prime})].

(40)

However, for arbitrary
Q_{i}
function (not as a function of
\pi_{i}
and
p_{i}
) that can be viewed as a matrix, the minimax theorem for normal-form game holds, such that

\displaystyle\max_{p_{i}}\min_{\pi_{i}}\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot|s,\mathbf{a})}[\pi_{i}^{T}(\cdot|s^{\prime})Q_{i}(s^{\prime},\cdot)p_{i}(\cdot|s^{\prime})-\frac{1}{\tau_{i}}D_{i}(p_{i},\pi_{-i};s^{\prime})+\epsilon_{i}\nu_{i}(\pi_{i};s^{\prime})]

(41)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min_{\pi_{i}}\max_{p_{i}}\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot|s,\mathbf{a})}[\pi_{i}^{T}(\cdot|s^{\prime})Q_{i}(s^{\prime},\cdot)p_{i}(\cdot|s^{\prime})-\frac{1}{\tau_{i}}D_{i}(p_{i},\pi_{-i};s^{\prime})+\epsilon_{i}\nu_{i}(\pi_{i};s^{\prime})]

so that
\mathcal{T}_{\pi_{i}^{*},\pi_{-i},p_{i}}
and
\mathcal{T}_{\pi_{i}^{*},\pi_{-i},p_{i}}^{\prime}
are indeed identical and share the same fixed point. Therefore, we claim that:

\min_{\pi_{i}}\max_{p_{i}}V_{i}^{\pi_{i},\pi_{-i},p_{i}}(s)=V_{i}^{\pi_{i}^{*},\pi_{-i},p_{i}^{*}}=V_{i}^{*,\pi_{-i}}=V_{i}^{\pi_{i}^{\dagger},\pi_{-i},p_{i}^{\dagger}}=\max_{p_{i}}\min_{\pi_{i}}V_{i}^{\pi_{i},\pi_{-i},p_{i}}(s)

(42)

∎

Having established the minimax theorem for Markov games, we now prove that similar to the normal-form case, that a Nash equilibrium of the 4-player Markov game corresponds to an RQE of the two-player game, and vice versa:

Lemma E.3
.

Let
(\pi^{*},p^{*})
be a Nash equilibrium of the 4-player Markov game, we have that
\pi^{*}
is an RQE of the original two-player Markov game. Furthermore, if
\pi^{*}
is an RQE of the two-player Markov game, then
(\pi^{*},p^{*})
is a Nash equilibrium of the 4-player Markov game where
p_{i}^{*}=\argmax_{p_{i}:\mathcal{S}\rightarrow\Delta_{|\mathcal{A}_{-i}|}}V_{i}^{\pi^{*},p_{i}}(s)
for all
s\in\mathcal{S}
.

Proof.

The first statement can be proved using a similar argument as in the proof of
Section

2.1
. Let
(\pi^{*},p^{*})
be a Nash equilibrium of the 4-player Markov game, by definition we have for all
\pi_{i}:\mathcal{S}\rightarrow\Delta_{|\mathcal{A}_{i}|}
and
s\in\mathcal{S}
:

V_{i}^{\pi^{*},p^{*}}(s)\leq V_{i}^{\pi_{i},\pi_{-i}^{*},p^{*}}(s),

(43)

and for all
p_{i}:\mathcal{S}\rightarrow\Delta_{|\mathcal{A}_{-i}|}
:

V_{i}^{\pi^{*},p_{i},p_{-i}^{*}}(s)\leq V_{i}^{\pi^{*},p^{*}}(s).

(44)

Therefore, we have:

\displaystyle f_{i}(\pi_{i}^{*},\pi_{-i}^{*};s)=

\displaystyle\max_{p_{i}:\mathcal{S}\rightarrow\Delta_{|\mathcal{A}_{-i}|}}V_{i}^{\pi^{*},p}(s)=V_{i}^{\pi^{*},p^{*}}(s)

(45)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle V_{i}^{\pi_{i},\pi_{-i}^{*},p^{*}}(s)\leq\max_{p_{i}^{\prime}:\mathcal{S}\rightarrow\Delta_{|\mathcal{A}_{-i}|}}V_{i}^{\pi_{i},\pi_{-i}^{*},p_{i}^{\prime}}(s)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f_{i}(\pi_{i},\pi_{-i}^{*};s).

For the second statement, let
\pi^{*}
be an RQE of the two-player Markov game, by the minimax theorem in Markov games, we have a similar argument as in
Section

2.1
that

\min_{\pi_{i}:\mathcal{S}\rightarrow\Delta_{|\mathcal{A}_{i}|}}\max_{p_{i}:\mathcal{S}\rightarrow\Delta_{|\mathcal{A}_{-i}|}}V_{i}^{\pi_{i},\pi_{-i}^{*},p_{i}}(s)=\min_{\pi_{i}:\mathcal{S}\rightarrow\Delta_{|\mathcal{A}_{i}|}}V_{i}^{\pi_{i},\pi_{-i}^{*},p_{i}^{*}}(s),

(46)

so that for all
s\in\mathcal{S}
,

V_{i}^{\pi^{*},p^{*}}(s)=\min_{\pi_{i}:\mathcal{S}\rightarrow\Delta_{|\mathcal{A}_{i}|}}\max_{p_{i}:\mathcal{S}\rightarrow\Delta_{|\mathcal{A}_{-i}|}}V_{i}^{\pi_{i},\pi_{-i}^{*},p_{i}}(s)=\min_{\pi_{i}:\mathcal{S}\rightarrow\Delta_{|\mathcal{A}_{i}|}}V_{i}^{\pi_{i},\pi_{-i}^{*},p_{i}^{*}}(s)\leq V_{i}^{\pi_{i}^{\prime},\pi_{-i}^{*},p_{i}^{*}}(s)

(47)

for all
\pi_{i}^{\prime}:\mathcal{S}\rightarrow\Delta_{|\mathcal{A}_{i}|}
, which completes the proof.
∎

With the auxiliary lemmas stated, we proceed to prove
Section

2.2
.

We first prove the first part. Let
\mathbf{Q}^{*}
be a fixed point of the Bellman optimality operator and
z^{*}=(\pi^{*},p^{*})
denote the minimax policies, we first prove that
\mathbf{Q}^{*}=\mathbf{Q}^{z^{*}}
. By the definition (
13
) of
\mathcal{T}
we have that for all
i\in\{1,2\}
and
(s,\mathbf{a})\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}
:

\displaystyle Q^{*}_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})

(48)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\mathcal{T}\mathbf{Q^{*}})_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-r_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})+\gamma\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot|s,\mathbf{a})}[\texttt{RQE}_{i}(-\mathbf{Q}^{*}(s^{\prime},\cdot))]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-r_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})+\gamma\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot|s,\mathbf{a})}\left[(\pi_{i}^{*})^{T}(\cdot|s^{\prime})Q^{*}_{i}(s^{\prime},\cdot)p^{*}_{i}(\cdot|s^{\prime})-\frac{1}{\tau_{i}}D_{i}(p^{*}_{i},\pi_{-i}^{*};s^{\prime})+\epsilon_{i}\nu_{i}(\pi_{i}^{*};s^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-r_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})+\gamma\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot|s,\mathbf{a})}\left[J_{i}\left(\pi^{*}_{i}(\cdot|s^{\prime}),\pi^{*}_{-i}(\cdot|s^{\prime}),p^{*}_{i}(\cdot|s^{\prime});-\mathbf{Q}^{*}(s^{\prime},\cdot)\right)\right]

We claim that
\mathbf{Q}^{*}=\mathbf{Q}^{z^{*}}
because we have the following for all
i\in[N],s\in\mathcal{S}
and
\mathbf{a}\in\mathcal{A}
:

\displaystyle Q^{*}_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})-Q^{z^{*}}_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})

(49)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\gamma\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot|s,\mathbf{a})}\left[J_{i}\left(\pi^{*}_{i}(\cdot|s^{\prime}),\pi^{*}_{-i}(\cdot|s^{\prime}),p^{*}_{i}(\cdot|s^{\prime});-\mathbf{Q}^{*}(s^{\prime},\cdot)\right)-J_{i}\left(\pi^{*}_{i}(\cdot|s^{\prime}),\pi^{*}_{-i}(\cdot|s^{\prime}),p^{*}_{i}(\cdot|s^{\prime});-\mathbf{Q}^{z^{*}}(s^{\prime},\cdot)\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\gamma\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot|s,\mathbf{a})}[(\pi_{i}^{*})^{T}(\cdot|s^{\prime})(Q^{*}_{i}-Q_{i}^{z^{*}})(s^{\prime},\cdot)p_{i}^{*}(\cdot|s^{\prime})]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\gamma\|\mathbf{Q}^{*}-\mathbf{Q}^{z^{*}}\|_{\max}.

Since this inequality holds for all
i,s\in\mathcal{S}
and
\mathbf{a}\in\mathcal{A}
, we know that
\|\mathbf{Q}^{*}-\mathbf{Q}^{z^{*}}\|_{\max}=0
.

Therefore, we have that for all policy
\pi_{i}
and all states
s\in\mathcal{S}
,

\displaystyle f_{i}(\pi_{i},\pi_{-i}^{*};s)=

\displaystyle\max_{p_{i}}V_{i}^{\pi_{i},\pi_{-i}^{*},p}(s)

(50)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{p_{i}}\pi_{i}(\cdot|s)^{T}Q_{i}^{\pi_{i},\pi_{-i}^{*},p}(s,\cdot)p_{i}(\cdot|s)-\frac{1}{\tau_{i}}D_{i}(p_{i},\pi^{*}_{-i};s)+\epsilon_{i}\nu_{i}(\pi_{i};s)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{\geq}}

\displaystyle\pi_{i}(\cdot|s)^{T}Q_{i}^{\pi_{i},\pi_{-i}^{*},p^{*}}(s,\cdot)p^{*}_{i}(\cdot|s)-\frac{1}{\tau_{i}}D_{i}(p^{*}_{i},\pi^{*}_{-i};s)+\epsilon_{i}\nu_{i}(\pi_{i};s)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(ii)}}}{{\geq}}

\displaystyle(\pi_{i}^{*})^{T}(\cdot|s)Q^{*}_{i}(s,\cdot)p^{*}_{i}(\cdot|s)-\frac{1}{\tau_{i}}D_{i}(p^{*}_{i},\pi^{*}_{-i};s)+\epsilon_{i}\nu_{i}(\pi^{*}_{i};s)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\texttt{RQE}_{i}(-\mathbf{Q}^{*}(s,\cdot))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\texttt{RQE}_{i}(-\mathbf{Q}^{z^{*}}(s,\cdot))

where (i) holds because we have taken maximum over
p_{i}
. Now we prove that (ii) holds. Notice that now
\pi_{-i}^{*}
and
p^{*}
are fixed, the problem becomes a single-agent MDP problem where the only policy to maximize is
\pi_{i}
. Recall that
\mathcal{T}_{*,\pi^{*}_{-i},p^{*}}
is the Bellman optimality operator acting on
Q_{i}
with respect to only
\pi_{i}
as follows:

\mathcal{T}_{*,\pi^{*}_{-i},p^{*}}Q_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})=-r_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})+\gamma\min_{\pi_{i}}\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot|s,\mathbf{a})}[\pi_{i}^{T}(\cdot|s^{\prime})Q_{i}(s^{\prime},\cdot)p^{*}_{i}(\cdot|s^{\prime})-\frac{1}{\tau_{i}}D_{i}(p^{*}_{i},\pi^{*}_{-i};s^{\prime})+\epsilon_{i}\nu_{i}(\pi_{i};s^{\prime})]

(51)

By construction of
\pi_{i}^{*}
we know that
\mathbf{Q}^{*}
is also a fixed point of
\mathcal{T}_{*,\pi^{*}_{-i},p^{*}}
as well. By standard result in single-agent MDPs and
\pi_{i}^{*}
is the corresponding optimal policy, we have:

V_{i}^{\pi_{i},\pi_{-i}^{*},p^{*}}(s)\geq V_{i}^{\pi_{i}^{*},\pi_{-i}^{*},p^{*}}(s),\forall s\in\mathcal{S},\pi_{i}:\mathcal{S}\rightarrow\Delta_{|\mathcal{A}_{i}|}

(52)

which immediately implies (ii) and completes the proof of the first part.

Now we proceed to prove the second part. Let
\pi^{*}
be an RQE of the two-player Markov game, we know by
Appendix

E
that
(\pi^{*},p^{*})
is a Nash equilibrium of the 4-player Markov game. By Lemma 10 in
(
Hu & Wellman 2003
)
, we know that
(\pi^{*},p^{*})
is also a Nash equilibrium of the stage game characterized by
\mathbf{Q}^{\pi^{*},p^{*}}(s,\cdot)
for all
s\in\mathcal{S}
. Therefore, by
Section

2.1
we know that
\pi^{*}
is an RQE of the two-player stage , which suggests that

V_{i}^{\pi^{*},p^{*}}(s)=\texttt{RQE}_{i}(-\mathbf{Q}^{\pi^{*},p^{*}}(s,\cdot))

(53)

This leads to the final result:

\displaystyle(\mathcal{T}\mathbf{Q}^{\pi^{*},p^{*}})_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})=

\displaystyle-r_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})+\gamma\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot|s,\mathbf{a})}[\texttt{RQE}_{i}(-\mathbf{Q}^{\pi^{*},p^{*}}(s^{\prime},\cdot))]

(54)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-r_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})+\gamma\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot|s,\mathbf{a})}[V_{i}^{\pi^{*},p^{*}}(s^{\prime})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle Q_{i}^{\pi^{*},p^{*}}(s,\mathbf{a}).

which completes the proof of
Section

2.2
.

Appendix F
Proofs and Additional Details for
Section

3

F.1
Proof of
Theorem

3.1

We follow a similar approach as in
(
Zhang & Mazumdar 2025
)
.

Proof of uniqueness.

Consider the KKT conditions for the objective functions (
6a
) and (
6b
) over the simplex of each player given
\mathbf{R}
, which must be satisfied at equilibrium point
z^{*}
. For the original player we have:

\displaystyle-R_{i}p_{i}^{*}+\epsilon_{i}\nabla\nu_{i}(\pi_{i}^{*})-\lambda(\pi_{i})+\mu(\pi_{i})1=0;

(55)

\displaystyle\pi_{i}^{*}\in\Delta_{|\mathcal{A}_{i}|};\lambda(\pi_{i})\geq 0;\mu(\pi_{i})\in\mathbb{R};\lambda(\pi_{i})^{T}\pi_{i}^{*}=0.

where
i\in\{1,2\}
,
\lambda(\pi_{i})
and
\mu(\pi_{i})
are Lagrange multipliers with respect to the simplex constraint, and
\lambda(\pi_{i})^{T}\pi_{i}^{*}=0
denotes complimentary slackness. For the adversaries we have:

\displaystyle R_{i}^{T}\pi_{i}^{*}+\frac{1}{\tau_{i}}\nabla_{p}D_{i}(p_{i}^{*},\pi_{-i}^{*})-\lambda(p_{i})+\mu(p_{i})1=0;

(56)

\displaystyle p_{i}^{*}\in\Delta_{|\mathcal{A}_{-i}|};\lambda(p_{i})\geq 0;\mu(p_{i})\in\mathbb{R};\lambda(p_{i})^{T}p_{i}^{*}=0.

where similarly
i\in\{1,2\}
,
\lambda(p_{i}),\mu(p_{i})
are Lagrange multipliers and
\lambda(p_{i})^{T}p_{i}^{*}=0
denotes complimentary slackness. We can combine (
55
) and (
56
) in a more compact form:

F(z^{*};\mathbf{R})=\begin{bmatrix}\lambda(\pi_{1})-\mu(\pi_{1})1\\
\lambda(\pi_{2})-\mu(\pi_{2})1\\
\lambda(p_{1})-\mu(p_{1})1\\
\lambda(p_{2})-\mu(p_{2})1\end{bmatrix}.

(57)

Therefore, for arbitrary
z\in\mathcal{Z}
we have:

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\langle z^{*}-z,F(z^{*};\mathbf{R})\rangle_{\lambda}

(58)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}\langle\pi_{1}^{*}-\pi_{1},\lambda(\pi_{1})-\mu(\pi_{1})1\rangle+\lambda_{2}\langle\pi_{2}^{*}-\pi_{2},\lambda(\pi_{2})-\mu(\pi_{2})1\rangle

\displaystyle+\lambda_{1}\langle p_{1}^{*}-p_{1},\lambda(p_{1})-\mu(p_{1})1\rangle+\lambda_{2}\langle p_{2}^{*}-p_{2},\lambda(p_{2})-\mu(p_{2})1\rangle

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(ii)}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}\langle\pi_{1}^{*}-\pi_{1},\lambda(\pi_{1})\rangle+\lambda_{2}\langle\pi_{2}^{*}-\pi_{2},\lambda(\pi_{2})\rangle+\lambda_{1}\langle p_{1}^{*}-p_{1},\lambda(p_{1})\rangle+\lambda_{2}\langle p_{2}^{*}-p_{2},\lambda(p_{2})\rangle

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(ii)}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle-\lambda_{1}\langle\pi_{1},\lambda(\pi_{1})\rangle-\lambda_{2}\langle\pi_{2},\lambda(\pi_{2})\rangle-\lambda_{1}\langle p_{1},\lambda(p_{1})\rangle-\lambda_{2}\langle p_{2},\lambda(p_{2})\rangle

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(iv)}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle 0

where (i) uses the definition of
\lambda
-weighted inner product, (ii) holds because
\pi_{i}\in\Delta_{|\mathcal{A}_{i}|},p_{i}\in\Delta_{|\mathcal{A}_{-i}|}
so that
\langle\pi^{*}_{i}-\pi_{i},1\rangle=0
and
\langle p^{*}_{i}-p_{i},1\rangle=0
, (iii) holds by complementary slackness and (iv) holds because
\lambda_{i},\pi_{i},p_{i},\lambda(\pi_{i}),\lambda(p_{i})>0
.

If
z_{1}^{*}
and
z_{2}^{*}
are two Nash equilibria of the 4-player game with respect to
\mathbf{R}
, we must have
\langle z_{1}-z_{2},F(z_{1};\mathbf{R})\rangle_{\lambda}\leq 0
and
\langle z_{2}-z_{1},F(z_{2};\mathbf{R})\rangle_{\lambda}\leq 0
. Adding these two inequalities up we have
\langle z_{1}-z_{2},F(z_{1};\mathbf{R})-F(z_{2};\mathbf{R})\rangle_{\lambda}\leq 0
, combining
\lambda
-strict monotonicity of
F(\cdot;\mathbf{R})
we have
z_{1}=z_{2}
, indicating the uniqueness of the Nash equilibrium.

Proof of Lipschitz Continuity.

In order to prove the Lipschitz continuity of RQE with respect to payoff matrices, let
\mathbf{R}
and
\mathbf{R}^{\prime}
be two pairs of payoff matrices, notice that (
58
) implies:

\langle z^{*}-z^{\dagger},F(z^{*};\mathbf{R})\rangle_{\lambda}\leq 0;\quad\langle z^{\dagger}-z^{*},F(z^{\dagger};\mathbf{R}^{\prime})\rangle_{\lambda}\leq 0.

(59)

adding these two inequalities up, we have:

\displaystyle\langle z^{*}-z^{\dagger},F(z^{*};\mathbf{R})-F(z^{\dagger};\mathbf{R}^{\prime})\rangle_{\lambda}\leq 0.

(60)

We split the left hand side into two difference terms and get:

\displaystyle\langle z^{*}-z^{\dagger},F(z^{*};\mathbf{R})-F(z^{*};\mathbf{R}^{\prime})\rangle_{\lambda}+\langle z^{*}-z^{\dagger},F(z^{*};\mathbf{R}^{\prime})-F(z^{\dagger};\mathbf{R}^{\prime})\rangle_{\lambda}\leq 0.

(61)

For the first term, we can use Cauchy-Schwarz inequality for
\lambda
-weighted norm:

\displaystyle\langle z^{*}-z^{\dagger},F(z^{*};\mathbf{R})-F(z^{*};\mathbf{R}^{\prime})\rangle_{\lambda}

(62)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle-\|\lambda\|_{\infty}\|z^{*}-z^{\dagger}\|_{2}\|F(z^{*};\mathbf{R})-F(z^{*};\mathbf{R}^{\prime})\|_{2}

For the second term,
(\mu,\lambda)
-strong monotonicity yields:

\displaystyle\langle z^{*}-z^{\dagger},F(z^{*};\mathbf{R}^{\prime})-F(z^{\dagger};\mathbf{R}^{\prime})\rangle_{\lambda}\geq\mu\|z^{*}-z^{\dagger}\|_{2}^{2}.

(63)

Therefore, combining the bounds above, we have:

\displaystyle\mu\|z^{*}-z^{\dagger}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\|\lambda\|_{\infty}\|z^{*}-z^{\dagger}\|_{2}\|F(z^{*};\mathbf{R})-F(z^{*};\mathbf{R}^{\prime})\|_{2},

(64)

canceling out
\|z^{*}-z^{\dagger}\|_{\lambda}
and rearranging terms we get:

\displaystyle\|z^{*}-z^{\dagger}\|_{2}\leq\|\lambda\|_{\infty}\|F(z^{*};\mathbf{R})-F(z^{*};\mathbf{R}^{\prime})\|_{2}/\mu.

(65)

Finally, by expanding
F(z^{*};\mathbf{R})-F(z^{*};\mathbf{R}^{\prime})
, we obtain:

\displaystyle\|F(z^{*};\mathbf{R})-F(z^{*};\mathbf{R}^{\prime})\|_{2}=\left\|\begin{bmatrix}-(R_{1}-R_{1}^{\prime})p_{1}^{*}\\
-(R_{2}-R_{2}^{\prime})p_{2}^{*}\\
(R_{1}-R_{1}^{\prime})^{T}\pi_{1}^{*}\\
(R_{2}-R_{2}^{\prime})^{T}\pi_{2}^{*}\end{bmatrix}\right\|_{2}\leq 2\left(\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{1}|}+\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{2}|}\right)\|\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\|_{\max},

(66)

and therefore,

\|z^{*}-z^{\dagger}\|_{2}\leq\frac{2\|\lambda\|_{\infty}\left(\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{1}|}+\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{2}|}\right)}{\mu}\|\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\|_{\max},

(67)

which completes the proof.

F.2
Proof of
Theorem

3.2

Part 1: General condition.

By
Appendix

I
, the monotonicity of the 4-player game (or equivalently, the monotonicity of the gradient operator
F
) can be characterized by the positivity of the operator
\Lambda\nabla F(z)+\nabla F(z)^{T}\Lambda
, where
\Lambda=\textup{Diag}(\lambda)
is a diagonal matrix with entries identical to
\lambda
. Recall the expression (
16
) of the gradient operator
F
, we can write the Jacobian of
F
as:

\nabla F(z)=\begin{bmatrix}\epsilon_{1}\nabla^{2}\nu_{1}&0&-R_{1}&0\\
0&\epsilon_{2}\nabla^{2}\nu_{2}&0&-R_{2}\\
R_{1}^{T}&\frac{1}{\tau_{1}}\nabla^{2}_{p\pi}D_{1}&\frac{1}{\tau_{1}}\nabla^{2}_{p}D_{1}&0\\
\frac{1}{\tau_{2}}\nabla^{2}_{p\pi}D_{2}&R_{2}^{T}&0&\frac{1}{\tau_{2}}\nabla^{2}_{p}D_{2}\end{bmatrix},

(68)

and therefore,
\Lambda\nabla F(z)
equals:

\Lambda\nabla F(z)=\begin{bmatrix}\lambda_{1}\epsilon_{1}\nabla^{2}\nu_{1}&0&-\lambda_{1}R_{1}&0\\
0&\lambda_{2}\epsilon_{2}\nabla^{2}\nu_{2}&0&-\lambda_{2}R_{2}\\
\lambda_{1}R_{1}^{T}&\frac{\lambda_{1}}{\tau_{1}}\nabla^{2}_{p\pi}D_{1}&\frac{\lambda_{1}}{\tau_{1}}\nabla^{2}_{p}D_{1}&0\\
\frac{\lambda_{2}}{\tau_{2}}\nabla^{2}_{p\pi}D_{2}&\lambda_{2}R_{2}^{T}&0&\frac{\lambda_{2}}{\tau_{2}}\nabla^{2}_{p}D_{2}\end{bmatrix}.

(69)

Similarly, we have:

\nabla F(z)^{T}\Lambda=\begin{bmatrix}\lambda_{1}\epsilon_{1}\nabla^{2}\nu_{1}&0&\lambda_{1}R_{1}&\frac{\lambda_{2}}{\tau_{2}}\nabla^{2}_{p\pi}D_{2}\\
0&\lambda_{2}\epsilon_{2}\nabla^{2}\nu_{2}&\frac{\lambda_{1}}{\tau_{1}}\nabla^{2}_{p\pi}D_{1}&\lambda_{2}R_{2}\\
-\lambda_{1}R_{1}^{T}&0&\frac{\lambda_{1}}{\tau_{1}}\nabla^{2}_{p}D_{1}&0\\
0&-\lambda_{2}R_{2}^{T}&0&\frac{\lambda_{2}}{\tau_{2}}\nabla^{2}_{p}D_{2}\end{bmatrix}

(70)

Combining the two expressions above, we obtain:

\Lambda\nabla F(z)+\nabla F(z)^{T}\Lambda=\begin{bmatrix}2\lambda_{1}\epsilon_{1}\nabla^{2}\nu_{1}&0&0&\frac{\lambda_{2}}{\tau_{2}}\nabla^{2}_{p\pi}D_{2}\\
0&2\lambda_{2}\epsilon_{2}\nabla^{2}\nu_{2}&\frac{\lambda_{1}}{\tau_{1}}\nabla^{2}_{p\pi}D_{1}&0\\
0&\frac{\lambda_{1}}{\tau_{1}}\nabla^{2}_{p\pi}D_{1}&2\frac{\lambda_{1}}{\tau_{1}}\nabla^{2}_{p}D_{1}&0\\
\frac{\lambda_{2}}{\tau_{2}}\nabla^{2}_{p\pi}D_{2}&0&0&2\frac{\lambda_{2}}{\tau_{2}}\nabla^{2}_{p}D_{2}\end{bmatrix}.

(71)

As a result, the condition stated in
Appendix

I
that
\Lambda\nabla F(z)+\nabla F(z)^{T}\Lambda\succeq 2\mu I
becomes:

\begin{bmatrix}2\lambda_{1}\epsilon_{1}\nabla^{2}\nu_{1}-2\mu I&0&0&\frac{\lambda_{2}}{\tau_{2}}\nabla^{2}_{p\pi}D_{2}\\
0&2\lambda_{2}\epsilon_{2}\nabla^{2}\nu_{2}-2\mu I&\frac{\lambda_{1}}{\tau_{1}}\nabla^{2}_{p\pi}D_{1}&0\\
0&\frac{\lambda_{1}}{\tau_{1}}\nabla^{2}_{p\pi}D_{1}&2\frac{\lambda_{1}}{\tau_{1}}\nabla^{2}_{p}D_{1}-2\mu I&0\\
\frac{\lambda_{2}}{\tau_{2}}\nabla^{2}_{p\pi}D_{2}&0&0&2\frac{\lambda_{2}}{\tau_{2}}\nabla^{2}_{p}D_{2}-2\mu I\end{bmatrix}\succeq 0

(72)

By rearranging rows and columns 2 and 4, we turn the matrix into block-diagonal form where positivity condition still holds:

\begin{bmatrix}2\lambda_{1}\epsilon_{1}\nabla^{2}\nu_{1}-2\mu I&\frac{\lambda_{2}}{\tau_{2}}\nabla^{2}_{p\pi}D_{2}&0&0\\
\frac{\lambda_{2}}{\tau_{2}}\nabla^{2}_{p\pi}D_{2}&2\frac{\lambda_{2}}{\tau_{2}}\nabla^{2}_{p}D_{2}-2\mu I&0&0\\
0&0&2\lambda_{2}\epsilon_{2}\nabla^{2}\nu_{2}-2\mu I&\frac{\lambda_{1}}{\tau_{1}}\nabla^{2}_{p\pi}D_{1}\\
0&0&\frac{\lambda_{1}}{\tau_{1}}\nabla^{2}_{p\pi}D_{1}&2\frac{\lambda_{1}}{\tau_{1}}\nabla^{2}_{p}D_{1}-2\mu I\\
\end{bmatrix}\succeq 0,

(73)

this simplifies to the positivity of the two diagonal blocks:

\begin{bmatrix}2\lambda_{1}\epsilon_{1}\nabla^{2}\nu_{1}-2\mu I&\frac{\lambda_{2}}{\tau_{2}}\nabla^{2}_{p\pi}D_{2}\\
\frac{\lambda_{2}}{\tau_{2}}\nabla^{2}_{p\pi}D_{2}&2\frac{\lambda_{2}}{\tau_{2}}\nabla^{2}_{p}D_{2}-2\mu I\\
\end{bmatrix}\succeq 0;\begin{bmatrix}2\lambda_{2}\epsilon_{2}\nabla^{2}\nu_{2}-2\mu I&\frac{\lambda_{1}}{\tau_{1}}\nabla^{2}_{p\pi}D_{1}\\
\frac{\lambda_{1}}{\tau_{1}}\nabla^{2}_{p\pi}D_{1}&2\frac{\lambda_{1}}{\tau_{1}}\nabla^{2}_{p}D_{1}-2\mu I\\
\end{bmatrix}\succeq 0.

(74)

This concludes the proof of part 1 in
Theorem

3.2
.

More discussion:
We can see that a necessary condition for these two matrices to be positive semidefinite is:

\nabla^{2}\nu_{i}\succeq\frac{\mu}{\epsilon_{i}\lambda_{i}}I;\quad\nabla_{p}^{2}D_{i}\succeq\frac{\mu\tau_{i}}{\lambda_{i}}I,\quad i\in\{1,2\}.

(75)

Notice that we further have:

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}2\lambda_{i}\epsilon_{i}\nabla^{2}\nu_{i}-2\mu I&\frac{\lambda_{-i}}{\tau_{-i}}\nabla^{2}_{p\pi}D_{-i}\\
\frac{\lambda_{-i}}{\tau_{-i}}\nabla^{2}_{p\pi}D_{-i}&2\frac{\lambda_{-i}}{\tau_{-i}}\nabla^{2}_{p}D_{-i}-2\mu I\\
\end{bmatrix}

(76)

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda_{-i}}{\tau_{-i}}\begin{bmatrix}\nabla^{2}_{\pi}D_{-i}&\nabla^{2}_{p\pi}D_{-i}\\
\nabla^{2}_{p\pi}D_{-i}&\nabla^{2}_{p}D_{-i}\end{bmatrix}+\begin{bmatrix}2\lambda_{i}\epsilon_{i}\nabla^{2}\nu_{i}-\frac{\lambda_{-i}}{\tau_{-i}}\nabla^{2}_{\pi}D_{-i}-2\mu I&0\\
0&\frac{\lambda_{-i}}{\tau_{-i}}\nabla^{2}_{p}D_{-i}-2\mu I\end{bmatrix},

where the first term is always positive by joint convexity of
D_{1}
. Therefore, a sufficient condition for this matrix to be positive semidefinite is:

\frac{\lambda_{-i}}{\tau_{-i}}\nabla^{2}_{p}D_{-i}-2\mu I\succeq 0;\quad 2\lambda_{i}\epsilon_{i}\nabla^{2}\nu_{i}-\frac{\lambda_{-i}}{\tau_{-i}}\nabla^{2}_{\pi}D_{-i}-2\mu I\succeq 0,\quad i\in\{1,2\}.

(77)

If there exists some
\mu>0
such that the conditions above are satisfied, we have:

2\lambda_{i}\epsilon_{i}\nabla^{2}\nu_{i}-\frac{\lambda_{-i}}{\tau_{-i}}\nabla^{2}_{\pi}D_{-i}\succeq 0.

(78)

Part 1: Log-barrier and KL

If
\nu_{i}(\cdot)
are log-barrier function and
D_{i}(\cdot,\cdot)
are KL divergence, we have that:

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}\nu_{i}(\pi_{i})=\textup{diag}(\pi_{i})^{-2};\nabla_{\pi_{-i}}^{2}D_{i}(p_{i},\pi_{-i})=\textup{diag}(p_{i})\textup{diag}(\pi_{-i})^{-2};

(79)

\displaystyle\nabla_{p_{i}}^{2}D_{i}(p_{i},\pi_{-i})=\textup{diag}(p_{i})^{-1};\nabla_{p_{i},\pi_{-i}}^{2}D_{i}(p_{i},\pi_{-i})=-\textup{diag}(\pi_{-i})^{-1}.

Therefore, the original condition:

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}2\lambda_{i}\epsilon_{i}\nabla^{2}\nu_{i}&\frac{\lambda_{-i}}{\tau_{-i}}\nabla^{2}_{p\pi}D_{-i}^{T}\\
\frac{\lambda_{-i}}{\tau_{-i}}\nabla^{2}_{p\pi}D_{-i}&2\frac{\lambda_{-i}}{\tau_{-i}}\nabla_{p}^{2}D_{-i}\end{bmatrix}\succeq 2\mu I

(80)

can be turned using Schur complement into:

\frac{\lambda_{-i}}{\tau_{-i}}\textup{diag}(p_{i})^{-1}-\mu I\succeq\frac{\lambda_{-i}^{2}}{4\tau_{-i}^{2}}\textup{diag}(\pi_{i})^{-1}\left(\lambda_{i}\epsilon_{i}\textup{diag}(\pi_{i})^{-2}-\mu I\right)^{-1}\textup{diag}(\pi_{i})^{-1}

(81)

this can be further rearranged into:

\left(\frac{\lambda_{-i}}{\tau_{-i}}\textup{diag}(p_{i})^{-1}-\mu I\right)\left(\lambda_{i}\epsilon_{i}\textup{diag}(\pi_{i})^{-2}-\mu I\right)\succeq\frac{\lambda_{-i}^{2}}{4\tau_{-i}^{2}}\textup{diag}(\pi_{i})^{-2}.

(82)

To guarantee there exists
\mu>0
such that the above condition holds, it suffices to have (taking
\mu\rightarrow 0
):

\lambda_{i}\epsilon_{i}\textup{diag}(p_{i})^{-1}\succ\frac{\lambda_{-i}}{4\tau_{-i}}I,

(83)

and since
\textup{diag}(p_{i})\prec I
, the condition can be simplified as:

4\epsilon_{i}\tau_{-i}\geq\frac{\lambda_{-i}}{\lambda_{i}}.

(84)

If we only require there exists
some

\lambda
satisfying this condition, we would only need:

4\epsilon_{1}\tau_{2}\geq\frac{\lambda_{2}}{\lambda_{1}}\geq\frac{1}{4\epsilon_{2}\tau_{1}},

(85)

that is,
16\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}\tau_{1}\tau_{2}>1
.

Part 2: Negative entropy and reverse KL.

If
\nu_{i}(\cdot)
are negative entropy and
D_{i}(\cdot,\cdot)
are reverse KL divergence, we have that:

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}\nu_{i}(\pi_{i})=\textup{diag}(\pi_{i})^{-1};\nabla_{\pi_{-i}}^{2}D_{i}(p_{i},\pi_{-i})=\textup{diag}(\pi_{-i})^{-1};

(86)

\displaystyle\nabla_{p_{i}}^{2}D_{i}(p_{i},\pi_{-i})=\textup{diag}(\pi_{-i})\textup{diag}(p_{i})^{-2};\nabla_{p_{i},\pi_{-i}}^{2}D_{i}(p_{i},\pi_{-i})=-\textup{diag}(p_{i})^{-1}.

Following a similar argument we have:

\left(\frac{\lambda_{-i}}{\tau_{-i}}\textup{diag}(\pi_{i})\textup{diag}(p_{i})^{-2}-\mu I\right)\left(\lambda_{i}\epsilon_{i}\textup{diag}(\pi_{i})^{-1}-\mu I\right)\succeq\frac{\lambda_{-i}^{2}}{4\tau_{-i}^{2}}\textup{diag}(p_{i})^{-2}

(87)

so we get the same strict monotonicity condition
4\epsilon_{i}\tau_{-i}>\frac{\lambda_{-i}}{\lambda_{i}}
. Therefore, as long as
16\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}\tau_{1}\tau_{2}>1
, there exists
\lambda
such that the game is strictly monotone.

Appendix G
Proofs for
Section

4

G.1
Detailed Statement and Proof of
Section

4.1

We now provide a more detailed version of
Section

4.1
as follows:

Proposition G.1

(
Section

4.1
, detailed)
.

If the 4-player stage game specified by (
11
) is
\mu
-strongly monotone for every state
s\in\mathcal{S}
(notice that whether a stage game is monotone does not depend on the
Q
functions but only the regularizers), then we have the following:

1.

If the regularizers
D_{i}(\cdot,\cdot)
are
L_{D}
-Lipschitz metrics that satisfy triangle inequality, and the discount factor
\gamma
satisfies:

\gamma\leq\frac{\tau_{\min}\mu}{\tau_{\min}\mu+2\|\lambda\|_{\infty}L_{D}(\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{1}|}+\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{2}|})},

(88)

where
\tau_{\min}=\min\{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}\}
, then the Bellman optimality operator
\mathcal{T}
is a
\gamma_{0}
-contraction mapping for
\gamma_{0}=\gamma\left(1+\frac{2\|\lambda\|_{\infty}L_{D}\left(\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{1}|}+\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{2}|}\right)}{\tau_{\min}\mu}\right)
.

2.

If
D_{i}(\cdot,\cdot)
are KL-divergence and
\nu_{i}(\cdot)
are log-barrier functions, given

\gamma_{0}=\gamma\left(1+2\|\lambda\|_{\infty}\left(|\mathcal{A}_{\max}|+\frac{\max\{\textup{sp}(\mathbf{Q}),\textup{sp}(\mathbf{Q}^{\prime})\}}{\epsilon_{\min}}\right)\frac{\left(\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{1}|}+\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{2}|}\right)}{\tau_{\min}^{2}\mu}\right)<1,

(89)

where
\epsilon_{\min}=\min\{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}\}
and
|\mathcal{A}_{\max}|=\max\{|\mathcal{A}_{1}|,|\mathcal{A}_{2}|\}
, then the Bellman optimality operator
\mathcal{T}
is a
\gamma_{0}
-contraction mapping.

Proof.

For two
Q
function pairs
\mathbf{Q}
and
\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}
, we have:

(\mathcal{T}\mathbf{Q}-\mathcal{T}\mathbf{Q}^{\prime})_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})\leq\gamma\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot|s,\mathbf{a})}\left[\texttt{RQE}_{i}(\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}(s^{\prime},\cdot))-\texttt{RQE}_{i}(\mathbf{Q}(s^{\prime},\cdot))\right]

(90)

For two different payoff matrices
\mathbf{R}
and
\mathbf{R}^{\prime}
,
(\pi^{*},p^{*})
and
(\pi^{\dagger},p^{\dagger})
are the RQEs w.r.t.
\mathbf{R}
and
\mathbf{R}^{\prime}
respectively, we have the following bound for the RQE difference term:

\displaystyle\texttt{RQE}_{i}(\mathbf{R})-\texttt{RQE}_{i}(\mathbf{R}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(\pi_{i}^{*})^{T}R_{i}p^{*}_{i}-\frac{1}{\tau_{i}}D_{i}(p_{i}^{*},\pi_{-i}^{*})+\epsilon_{i}\nu_{i}(\pi_{i}^{*})+(\pi_{i}^{\dagger})^{T}R_{i}^{\prime}p^{\dagger}_{i}+\frac{1}{\tau_{i}}D_{i}(p_{i}^{\dagger},\pi_{-i}^{\dagger})-\epsilon_{i}\nu_{i}(\pi_{i}^{\dagger})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-(\pi_{i}^{\dagger})^{T}R_{i}p^{*}_{i}-\frac{1}{\tau_{i}}D_{i}(p_{i}^{*},\pi_{-i}^{*})+\epsilon_{i}\nu_{i}(\pi_{i}^{\dagger})+(\pi_{i}^{\dagger})^{T}R_{i}^{\prime}p^{\dagger}_{i}+\frac{1}{\tau_{i}}D_{i}(p_{i}^{\dagger},\pi_{-i}^{\dagger})-\epsilon_{i}\nu_{i}(\pi_{i}^{\dagger})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\pi_{i}^{\dagger})^{T}(R_{i}^{\prime}p^{\dagger}_{i}-R_{i}p_{i}^{*})-\frac{1}{\tau_{i}}D_{i}(p_{i}^{*},\pi_{-i}^{*})+\frac{1}{\tau_{i}}D_{i}(p_{i}^{\dagger},\pi_{-i}^{\dagger})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(\pi_{i}^{\dagger})^{T}(R_{i}^{\prime}-R_{i})p_{i}^{*}-\frac{1}{\tau_{i}}D_{i}(p_{i}^{*},\pi_{-i}^{*})+\frac{1}{\tau_{i}}D_{i}(p_{i}^{*},\pi_{-i}^{\dagger})

with the inequalities follow from
Section

2.1
.

Proof for Part 1.

We first consider the case that
D_{i}
are
L_{D}
-Lipschitz and satisfies triangle inequality, by
Theorem

3.1
we have:

\displaystyle\texttt{RQE}_{i}(\mathbf{R})-\texttt{RQE}_{i}(\mathbf{R}^{\prime})

(91)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(\pi_{i}^{\dagger})^{T}(R_{i}^{\prime}-R_{i})p_{i}^{*}+\frac{1}{\tau_{i}}D_{i}(\pi_{-i}^{*},\pi_{-i}^{\dagger})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(\pi_{i}^{\dagger})^{T}(R_{i}^{\prime}-R_{i})p_{i}^{*}+\frac{L_{D}}{\tau_{i}}\|\pi_{-i}^{*}-\pi_{-i}^{\dagger}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(1+\frac{2\|\lambda\|_{\infty}L_{D}\left(\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{1}|}+\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{2}|}\right)}{\tau_{i}\mu}\right)\|\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\|_{\max}.

Now we take max-norm with respect to all possible
(s,\mathbf{a})
pairs in (
90
) and obtain:

\displaystyle\|(\mathcal{T}\mathbf{Q}-\mathcal{T}\mathbf{Q}^{\prime})_{i}\|_{\max}\leq\gamma\left(1+\frac{2\|\lambda\|_{\infty}L_{D}\left(\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{1}|}+\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{2}|}\right)}{\tau_{i}\mu}\right)\|\mathbf{Q}-\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\|_{\max}

(92)

since this upper bound holds for both
i\in\{1,2\}
, the left hand side can be simply rewritten as
\|\mathcal{T}\mathbf{Q}-\mathcal{T}\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\|_{\max}
, therefore
\mathcal{T}
is a contraction when

\gamma\left(1+\frac{2\|\lambda\|_{\infty}L_{D}\left(\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{1}|}+\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{2}|}\right)}{\tau_{i}\mu}\right)\leq 1.

(93)

Proof for Part 2.

For the case where
D_{i}
are KL-divergence and
\nu_{i}
are log-barrier functions, from
Appendix

I
we obtain that
\pi^{*},\pi^{\dagger}
are bounded below by
\underline{\pi}=\frac{\epsilon_{i}}{\epsilon_{i}|\mathcal{A}_{i}|+\max\{\textup{sp}(\mathbf{R}),\textup{sp}(\mathbf{R}^{\prime})\}}
, and therefore:

\displaystyle D_{i}(p_{i}^{*},\pi_{-i}^{*})-D_{i}(p_{i}^{*},\pi_{-i}^{\dagger})

(94)

\displaystyle=(p_{i}^{*})^{T}(\log\pi_{-i}^{\dagger}-\log\pi_{-i}^{*})

\displaystyle\leq\|p_{i}^{*}\|_{2}\|\frac{\pi_{-i}^{\dagger}-\pi_{-i}^{*}}{\underline{\pi}}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\underline{\pi}}\|\pi_{-i}^{\dagger}-\pi_{-i}^{*}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left(|\mathcal{A}_{i}|+\frac{\max\{\textup{sp}(\mathbf{R}),\textup{sp}(\mathbf{R}^{\prime})\}}{\epsilon_{i}}\right)\|\pi_{-i}^{\dagger}-\pi_{-i}^{*}\|_{2},

this gives the result that:

\displaystyle\texttt{RQE}_{i}(\mathbf{R})-\texttt{RQE}_{i}(\mathbf{R}^{\prime})

(95)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(\pi_{i}^{\dagger})^{T}(R_{i}^{\prime}-R_{i})p_{i}^{*}-\frac{1}{\tau_{i}}D_{i}(p_{i}^{*},\pi_{-i}^{*})+\frac{1}{\tau_{i}}D_{i}(p_{i}^{*},\pi_{-i}^{\dagger})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(\pi_{i}^{\dagger})^{T}(R_{i}^{\prime}-R_{i})p_{i}^{*}+\left(\frac{|\mathcal{A}_{i}|}{\tau_{i}}+\frac{\max\{\textup{sp}(\mathbf{R}),\textup{sp}(\mathbf{R}^{\prime})\}}{\epsilon_{i}\tau_{i}}\right)\|\pi_{-i}^{\dagger}-\pi_{-i}^{*}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\|_{\max}+\|\lambda\|_{\infty}\left(\frac{|\mathcal{A}_{i}|}{\tau_{i}}+\frac{\max\{\textup{sp}(\mathbf{R}),\textup{sp}(\mathbf{R}^{\prime})\}}{\epsilon_{i}\tau_{i}}\right)\frac{2\left(\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{1}|}+\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{2}|}\right)}{\tau_{\min}\mu}\|\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\|_{\max}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(1+2\|\lambda\|_{\infty}\left(|\mathcal{A}_{i}|+\frac{\max\{\textup{sp}(\mathbf{R}),\textup{sp}(\mathbf{R}^{\prime})\}}{\epsilon_{\min}}\right)\frac{\left(\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{1}|}+\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{2}|}\right)}{\tau_{\min}^{2}\mu}\right)\|\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\|_{\max},

so that

\|\mathcal{T}\mathbf{Q}-\mathcal{T}\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\|_{\max}\leq\gamma\left(1+2\|\lambda\|_{\infty}\left(|\mathcal{A}_{\max}|+\frac{\max\{\textup{sp}(\mathbf{Q}),\textup{sp}(\mathbf{Q}^{\prime})\}}{\epsilon_{\min}}\right)\frac{\left(\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{1}|}+\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{2}|}\right)}{\tau_{\min}^{2}\mu}\right)\|\mathbf{Q}-\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\|_{\max}.

(96)

which completes the proof.
∎

G.2
Detailed Statement and Proof of
Theorem

4.4

We first provide a detailed statement of
Theorem

4.4
as follows:

Theorem G.2

(
Theorem

4.4
, detailed)
.

If there exists
\gamma_{0}
such that the Bellman optimality operator
\mathcal{T}
is a contraction mapping, and additionally
D_{i}(\cdot,\cdot)
are
L_{D}
-Lipschitz in either argument when the other is fixed,
\nu_{i}(\cdot)
are
L_{\nu}
-Lipschitz with respect to its input, and the gradient operator of the modified 4-player game is
(\mu,\lambda)
-strongly monotone and
L_{F}
-Lipschitz for every state
s\in\mathcal{S}
, let

C_{\mathcal{T}}=\gamma(\frac{\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{\max}|}}{2(1-\gamma)}\left(1+\gamma(\frac{2\sqrt{2}L_{D}}{\tau_{\min}}+\sqrt{2}\epsilon_{\max}L_{\nu})\right)+\frac{L_{D}}{\tau_{\min}}+\max\{\frac{L_{D}}{\tau_{\min}},\epsilon_{\max}L_{\nu}\})

(97)

then (
17
) with a two-timescale stepsize choice satisfying the following:

\displaystyle\beta_{t}\leq\min\{\frac{1}{\mu},\frac{\mu}{L_{F}^{2}}\};

(98)

\displaystyle\frac{\beta_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}\geq\left((1-\gamma_{0})+2L_{RQE}C_{\mathcal{T}}\left(1-\gamma_{0}+2\frac{1+\gamma_{0}}{1-\gamma_{0}}\right)\right).

where
L_{RQE}
is the Lipschitz continuity constant of RQE with respect to the payoff matrix in normal form games as indicated by
Theorem

3.1
,
then the sequence of
z_{t}
converges to the RQE
z^{*}
of the Markov game and the
Q
function
\mathbf{Q}_{t}
converges to the corresponding
\mathbf{Q}^{*}
to
z^{*}
at the following rates:

1.

If we use constant step sizes
\alpha_{t}=\alpha,\beta_{t}=\beta
, then
\forall s\in\mathcal{S}
:

\displaystyle\|z_{t}(\cdot|s)-z^{*}(\cdot|s)\|_{2}\leq\left(1-\frac{\tilde{\alpha}}{2}\right)^{t}\left(1+\frac{L_{RQE}}{C_{2}}\right)\left(\left(\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{1}|}+\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{2}|}\right)+C_{2}Q_{\max}\right);

(99)

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathbf{Q}^{*}\|_{\max}\leq\left(1-\frac{\tilde{\alpha}}{2}\right)^{t}\left(\frac{1}{C_{2}}\left(\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{1}|}+\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{2}|}\right)+Q_{\max}\right).

where
\tilde{\alpha}=(1-\gamma_{0})\alpha
,
C_{2}=\frac{1+\gamma_{0}}{1-\gamma_{0}}L_{RQE}
, and

Q_{\max}=\frac{1}{1-\gamma}+\frac{\gamma}{1-\gamma}\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{\min}}(D_{\min}+2\sqrt{2}L_{D})+\epsilon_{\max}\left(\nu_{\min}+\sqrt{2}L_{\nu}\right)\right).

(100)

2.

If we use diminishing step sizes
\alpha_{t}=\frac{\alpha}{t+h}
and
\beta_{t}=\frac{\beta}{t+h}
, then
\forall s\in\mathcal{S}
:

\displaystyle\|z_{t}(\cdot|s)-z^{*}(\cdot|s)\|_{2}\leq\left(1+\frac{L_{RQE}}{C_{2}}\right)\left(\left(\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{1}|}+\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{2}|}\right)+C_{2}Q_{\max}\right)\left(\frac{h}{h+t+1}\right)^{\frac{\tilde{\alpha}}{2}};

(101)

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathbf{Q}^{*}\|_{\max}\leq\left(\frac{1}{C_{2}}\left(\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{1}|}+\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{2}|}\right)+Q_{\max}\right)\left(\frac{h}{h+t+1}\right)^{\frac{\tilde{\alpha}}{2}}.

where
\tilde{\alpha},C_{2}
and
Q_{\max}
the same as above.

Although the conditions of
Theorem

G.2
look stronger than
Theorem

4.4
, the condition that
D_{i}(\cdot,\cdot)
and
\nu_{i}(\cdot)
are both smooth indeed implies the conditions in
Theorem

G.2
. This is because the domains of
D_{i}
and
\nu_{i}
are both compact sets, where smoothness implies they are Lipschitz continuous, and
Appendix

I
implies the Lipschitz continuity of
F
as well.

Proof of
Theorem

G.2
.

For notational simplicity, we use
F_{t}(\cdot)
to denote
F(\cdot;-\mathbf{Q}_{t})
. Let
z_{t}^{*}(\cdot|s)
denote the state-wise RQE induced by
\mathbf{Q}_{t}(s,\cdot)
. We first prove that the
Q
iterations are bounded throughout:

Lemma G.3
.

If
D_{i}(\cdot,\cdot)
are
L_{D}
-Lipschitz and
\nu_{i}(\cdot)
are
L_{\nu}
-Lipschitz, then we have:

\textup{sp}(\mathbf{Q}_{t})\leq Q_{span},\quad\forall t.\quad\textup{sp}(\mathbf{Q}^{*})\leq Q_{span}.

(102)

where

Q_{span}=\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\left(1+\gamma(\frac{2\sqrt{2}L_{D}}{\tau_{\min}}+\sqrt{2}\epsilon_{\max}L_{\nu})\right).

(103)

Additionally, we have:

\|\mathbf{Q}_{t}\|_{\max}\leq Q_{\max},\quad\forall t.\quad\|\mathbf{Q}^{*}\|_{\max}\leq Q_{\max}.

(104)

where

Q_{\max}=\frac{1}{1-\gamma}+\frac{\gamma}{1-\gamma}\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{\min}}(D_{\min}+2\sqrt{2}L_{D})+\epsilon_{\max}\left(\nu_{\min}+\sqrt{2}L_{\nu}\right)\right).

(105)

Proof.

We provide proofs for span and max-norm respectively.

Result for span of
Q
.

By the update rule

\mathbf{Q}_{t+1}=(1-\alpha_{t})\mathbf{Q}_{t}+\alpha_{t}\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}\mathbf{Q}_{t}

(106)

we have that

\displaystyle\textup{sp}(\mathbf{Q}_{t+1})\leq(1-\alpha_{t})\textup{sp}(\mathbf{Q}_{t})+\alpha_{t}\textup{sp}(\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}\mathbf{Q}_{t})

(107)

for the latter term, recall the definition (
14
) of
\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}
, we have:

\displaystyle\textup{sp}(\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}\mathbf{Q}_{t})

(108)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{i,s}\left(\max_{\mathbf{a}}(\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}\mathbf{Q}_{t})_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})-\min_{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}\mathbf{Q}_{t})_{i}(s,\mathbf{a}^{\prime})\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max_{i,s}\Bigg(\max_{\mathbf{a}}\{-r_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})+\gamma\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot|s,\mathbf{a})}[\pi_{i,t+1}^{T}(\cdot|s^{\prime})Q_{i,t}(s^{\prime},\cdot)p_{i,t+1}(\cdot|s^{\prime})-\frac{1}{\tau_{i}}D_{i}(p_{i,t+1},\pi_{-i,t+1};s^{\prime})+\epsilon_{i}\nu_{i}(\pi_{i,t+1};s^{\prime})]\}

\displaystyle-\min_{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}}\{-r_{i}(s,\mathbf{a}^{\prime})+\gamma\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot|s,\mathbf{a}^{\prime})}[\pi_{i,t+1}^{T}(\cdot|s^{\prime})Q_{i,t}(s^{\prime},\cdot)p_{i,t+1}(\cdot|s^{\prime})-\frac{1}{\tau_{i}}D_{i}(p_{i,t+1},\pi_{-i,t+1};s^{\prime})+\epsilon_{i}\nu_{i}(\pi_{i,t+1};s^{\prime})]\}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 1+\gamma\max_{i}\left(\max_{s,\mathbf{a}}Q_{i,t}(s,\mathbf{a})-\min_{s,\mathbf{a}}Q_{i,t}(s,\mathbf{a})\right)+\gamma\max_{i}\frac{1}{\tau_{i}}\left(\max_{p,\pi}D_{i}(p,\pi)-\min_{p,\pi}D_{i}(p,\pi)\right)

\displaystyle+\gamma\max_{i}\epsilon_{i}\left(\max_{\pi}\nu_{i}(\pi)-\min_{\pi}\nu_{i}(\pi)\right)

Since
\nu_{i}(\cdot)
are
L_{\nu}
-Lipschitz and
\pi\in\Delta
, we have:

\max_{\pi}\nu_{i}(\pi)-\min_{\pi}\nu_{i}(\pi)\leq\sqrt{2}L_{\nu},

(109)

and similarly,

\max_{p,\pi}D_{i}(p,\pi)-\min_{p,\pi}D_{i}(p,\pi)\leq 2\sqrt{2}L_{D}

(110)

therefore, we have:

\textup{sp}(\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}\mathbf{Q}_{t})\leq 1+\gamma\textup{sp}(\mathbf{Q}_{t})+\gamma(\frac{2\sqrt{2}L_{D}}{\tau_{\min}}+\sqrt{2}\epsilon_{\max}L_{\nu})

(111)

Therefore, we have:

\displaystyle\textup{sp}(\mathbf{Q}_{t+1})\leq

\displaystyle(1-\alpha_{t})\textup{sp}(\mathbf{Q}_{t})+\alpha_{t}\textup{sp}(\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}\mathbf{Q}_{t})

(112)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(1-(1-\gamma)\alpha_{t})\textup{sp}(\mathbf{Q}_{t})+\alpha_{t}\left(1+\gamma(\frac{2\sqrt{2}L_{D}}{\tau_{\min}}+\sqrt{2}\epsilon_{\max}L_{\nu})\right)

since
\mathbf{Q}_{0}=0
, using a simple induction argument we obtain:

\textup{sp}(\mathbf{Q}_{t})\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\left(1+\gamma(\frac{2\sqrt{2}L_{D}}{\tau_{\min}}+\sqrt{2}\epsilon_{\max}L_{\nu})\right),\quad\forall t.

(113)

For
\textup{sp}(\mathbf{Q}^{*})
, notice that
\mathbf{Q}^{*}=\mathcal{T}_{z^{*}}\mathbf{Q}^{*}
and the span bound holds for all
z_{t+1}
gives the result.

Result for max-norm of
Q
.

Similarly by the update rule, we only have to control
\|\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}\mathbf{Q}_{t}\|_{\max}
. We have:

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}\mathbf{Q}_{t}\|_{\max}

(114)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{i,s,\mathbf{a}}\{|-r_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})+\gamma\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot|s,\mathbf{a})}[\pi_{i,t+1}^{T}(\cdot|s^{\prime})Q_{i,t}(s^{\prime},\cdot)p_{i,t+1}(\cdot|s^{\prime})-\frac{1}{\tau_{i}}D_{i}(p_{i,t+1},\pi_{-i,t+1};s^{\prime})+\epsilon_{i}\nu_{i}(\pi_{i,t+1};s^{\prime})]|\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\gamma\left(1+\|\mathbf{Q}_{t}\|_{\max}+\frac{1}{\tau_{\min}}(D_{\min}+2\sqrt{2}L_{D})+\epsilon_{\max}\left(\nu_{\min}+\sqrt{2}L_{\nu}\right)\right)

where we have used (
109
) and (
110
) in the inequality. Using the same reasoning as above, we have:

\|\mathbf{Q}_{t}\|_{\max}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}+\frac{\gamma}{1-\gamma}\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{\min}}(D_{\min}+2\sqrt{2}L_{D})+\epsilon_{\max}\left(\nu_{\min}+\sqrt{2}L_{\nu}\right)\right),

(115)

and using the same argument again, we have:

\|\mathbf{Q}^{*}\|_{\max}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}+\frac{\gamma}{1-\gamma}\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{\min}}(D_{\min}+2\sqrt{2}L_{D})+\epsilon_{\max}\left(\nu_{\min}+\sqrt{2}L_{\nu}\right)\right),

(116)

which completes the proof.
∎

We now focus on the
z
update. We have the following lemma on the update rule of
z_{t}
for arbitrary
s\in\mathcal{S}
, where we drop the dependence on
s
for simplicity:

Lemma G.4
.

Consider the following update rule on
z
:

z_{t+1}\leftarrow\textup{Proj}\left(z_{t}-\beta_{t}\Lambda F_{t}(z_{t})\right),

(117)

If
F_{t}
is
L_{F}
-Lipschitz continuous and
(\mu,\lambda)
-strongly monotone in
z
, we have the following:

\displaystyle\|z_{t+1}-z_{t}^{*}\|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{1-2\beta_{t}\mu+\beta_{t}^{2}L_{F}^{2}}\|z_{t}-z_{t}^{*}\|_{2};

(118)

\displaystyle\|z_{t+1}-z_{t+1}^{*}\|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{1-2\beta_{t}\mu+\beta_{t}^{2}L_{F}^{2}}\|z_{t}-z_{t}^{*}\|_{2}+\|z_{t+1}^{*}-z_{t}^{*}\|_{2}.

(119)

Proof.

For
\|z_{t+1}-z_{t}^{*}\|_{2}
, we obtain by our update rule and
Appendix

I
that:

\displaystyle\|z_{t+1}-z_{t}^{*}\|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\|\textup{Proj}(z_{t}-\beta_{t}\Lambda F_{t}(z_{t}))-\textup{Proj}(z_{t}^{*}-\beta_{t}\Lambda F_{t}(z_{t}^{*}))\|_{2}

(120)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|z_{t}-z_{t}^{*}-\beta_{t}\Lambda(F_{t}(z_{t})-F_{t}(z_{t}^{*}))\|_{2}

Notice that we further have:

\displaystyle\|z_{t}-z_{t}^{*}-\beta_{t}\Lambda(F_{t}(z_{t})-F_{t}(z_{t}^{*}))\|_{2}^{2}

(121)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|z_{t}-z_{t}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}-2\beta_{t}\langle z_{t}-z_{t}^{*},F_{t}(z_{t})-F_{t}(z_{t}^{*})\rangle_{\lambda}+\beta_{t}^{2}\|F_{t}(z_{t})-F_{t}(z_{t}^{*})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|z_{t}-z_{t}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}-2\beta_{t}\mu\|z_{t}-z_{t}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}+\beta_{t}^{2}L_{F}^{2}\|z_{t}-z_{t}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1-2\beta_{t}\mu+\beta_{t}^{2}L_{F}^{2})\|z_{t}-z_{t}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}

where we have used the
(\mu,\lambda)
-strong monotonicity of
F_{t}
and
L_{F}
-Lipschitz continuity of
F_{t}
in the inequality, we conclude that the first line of our results hold.

The second line holds by noticing that:

\displaystyle\|z_{t+1}-z_{t+1}^{*}\|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\|z_{t+1}-z_{t}^{*}\|_{2}+\|z_{t+1}^{*}-z_{t}^{*}\|_{2}

(122)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{1-2\beta_{t}\mu+\beta_{t}^{2}L_{F}^{2}}\|z_{t}-z_{t}^{*}\|_{2}+\|z_{t+1}^{*}-z_{t}^{*}\|_{2},

which completes the proof of
Section

G.2
.
∎

Notice that
z_{t+1}^{*}
is the state-wise RQE of
\mathbf{Q}_{t+1}
and
z_{t}^{*}
is the state-wise RQE of (the normal form)
\mathbf{Q}
, we have:

\displaystyle\|z^{*}_{t+1}-z^{*}_{t}\|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle L_{RQE}\|\mathbf{Q}_{t+1}-\mathbf{Q}_{t}\|_{\max}

(123)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\alpha_{t}L_{RQE}\|\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathbf{Q}_{t}\|_{\max}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\alpha_{t}L_{RQE}\left(\|\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathcal{T}\mathbf{Q}_{t}\|_{\max}+\|\mathcal{T}\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathbf{Q}_{t}\|_{\max}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\alpha_{t}L_{RQE}\left(\|\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathcal{T}_{z_{t}^{*}}\mathbf{Q}_{t}\|_{\max}+\|\mathcal{T}\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathbf{Q}_{t}\|_{\max}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\alpha_{t}L_{RQE}\left(\|\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathcal{T}_{z_{t}^{*}}\mathbf{Q}_{t}\|_{\max}+\|\mathcal{T}\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathcal{T}\mathbf{Q}^{*}\|_{\max}+\|\mathcal{T}\mathbf{Q}^{*}-\mathbf{Q}_{t}\|_{\max}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\alpha_{t}L_{RQE}\left(\|\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathcal{T}_{z_{t}^{*}}\mathbf{Q}_{t}\|_{\max}+\gamma_{0}\|\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathbf{Q}^{*}\|_{\max}+\|\mathbf{Q}^{*}-\mathbf{Q}_{t}\|_{\max}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\alpha_{t}L_{RQE}\left(\|\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathcal{T}_{z_{t}^{*}}\mathbf{Q}_{t}\|_{\max}+(1+\gamma_{0})\|\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathbf{Q}^{*}\|_{\max}\right),

where
L_{RQE}
is the Lipschitz continuity constant of RQE given by
Theorem

3.1
with respect to the difference term
\|z_{t+1}-z_{t}\|_{2}
.

Now we consider the iteration step of
\mathbf{Q}
. Let
\mathbf{Q}^{*}
be the
Q
function corresponding to the RQE of the Markov game, we have:

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{Q}_{t+1}-\mathbf{Q}^{*}\|_{\max}\leq

\displaystyle(1-\alpha_{t})\|\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathbf{Q}^{*}\|_{\max}+\alpha_{t}\|\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathcal{T}\mathbf{Q}_{t}\|_{\max}+\alpha_{t}\|\mathcal{T}\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathcal{T}\mathbf{Q}^{*}\|_{\max}

(124)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(1-(1-\gamma_{0})\alpha_{t}\right)\|\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathbf{Q}^{*}\|_{\max}+\alpha_{t}\|\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathcal{T}\mathbf{Q}_{t}\|_{\max}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(1-(1-\gamma_{0})\alpha_{t}\right)\|\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathbf{Q}^{*}\|_{\max}+\alpha_{t}\|\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathcal{T}_{z_{t}^{*}}\mathbf{Q}_{t}\|_{\max},

where we have used the
\gamma_{0}
-contraction property of
\mathcal{T}
in the second inequality, and the fact that
z_{t}^{*}
is the state-wise RQE of
\mathbf{Q}_{t}
. For the difference term
\|\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathcal{T}_{z_{t}^{*}}\mathbf{Q}_{t}\|_{\max}
, we have the following lemma:

Lemma G.5
.

Given a set of
Q
functions
\mathbf{Q}
, for arbitrary
z\in\Delta_{|\mathcal{A}_{1}|}\times\Delta_{|\mathcal{A}_{2}|}\times\Delta_{|\mathcal{A}_{2}|}\times\Delta_{|\mathcal{A}_{1}|}
, the following bound holds:

\|\mathcal{T}_{z}\mathbf{Q}-\mathcal{T}\mathbf{Q}\|_{\max}\leq\gamma(\frac{\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{\max}|}}{2}\textup{sp}(\mathbf{Q})+\frac{L_{D}}{\tau_{\min}}+\max\{\frac{L_{D}}{\tau_{\min}},\epsilon_{\max}L_{\nu}\})\|z-z^{*}\|_{2}

(125)

where
z^{*}
is the state-wise RQE of
\mathbf{Q}
and
L_{D},L_{\nu}
are the maximum Lipschitz continuity constant of
D_{i}
and
\nu_{i}
respectively.

Proof.

Given state-action pair
(s,\mathbf{a})
, let
z=(\pi_{1},\pi_{2},p_{1},p_{2})
and
z^{*}=(\pi_{1}^{*},\pi_{2}^{*},p_{1}^{*},p_{2}^{*})
, we have the following lower bound:

\displaystyle(\mathcal{T}_{z}\mathbf{Q}-\mathcal{T}\mathbf{Q})_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})

(126)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\gamma\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot|s,\mathbf{a})}[\pi_{i}^{T}Q_{i}(s^{\prime},\cdot)p_{i}-\frac{1}{\tau_{i}}D_{i}(p_{i},\pi_{-i};s^{\prime})+\epsilon_{i}\nu_{i}(\pi_{i};s^{\prime})]

\displaystyle-\gamma\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot|s,\mathbf{a})}[(\pi_{i}^{*})^{T}Q_{i}(s^{\prime},\cdot)p^{*}_{i}-\frac{1}{\tau_{i}}D_{i}(p^{*}_{i},\pi^{*}_{-i};s^{\prime})+\epsilon_{i}\nu_{i}(\pi^{*}_{i};s^{\prime})]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\gamma\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot|s,\mathbf{a})}[\pi_{i}^{T}Q_{i}(s^{\prime},\cdot)p_{i}-\frac{1}{\tau_{i}}D_{i}(p_{i},\pi_{-i};s^{\prime})+\epsilon_{i}\nu_{i}(\pi_{i};s^{\prime})]

\displaystyle-\gamma\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot|s,\mathbf{a})}[\pi_{i}^{T}Q_{i}(s^{\prime},\cdot)p^{*}_{i}-\frac{1}{\tau_{i}}D_{i}(p^{*}_{i},\pi^{*}_{-i};s^{\prime})+\epsilon_{i}\nu_{i}(\pi_{i};s^{\prime})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\gamma\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot|s,\mathbf{a})}[\pi_{i}^{T}Q_{i}(s^{\prime},\cdot)(p_{i}-p^{*}_{i})-\frac{1}{\tau_{i}}(D_{i}(p_{i},\pi_{-i};s^{\prime})-D_{i}(p^{*}_{i},\pi^{*}_{-i};s^{\prime}))]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle-\gamma(\frac{\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{-i}|}}{2}\textup{sp}(Q_{i})+\frac{2L_{D}}{\tau_{i}})\|z-z^{*}\|_{2},

and a similar upper bound:

\displaystyle(\mathcal{T}_{z}\mathbf{Q}-\mathcal{T}_{z^{*}}\mathbf{Q})_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})

(127)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\gamma\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot|s,\mathbf{a})}[\pi_{i}^{T}Q_{i}(s^{\prime},\cdot)p_{i}-\frac{1}{\tau_{i}}D_{i}(p_{i},\pi_{-i};s^{\prime})+\epsilon_{i}\nu_{i}(\pi_{i};s^{\prime})]

\displaystyle-\gamma\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot|s,\mathbf{a})}[(\pi_{i}^{*})^{T}Q_{i}(s^{\prime},\cdot)p^{*}_{i}-\frac{1}{\tau_{i}}D_{i}(p^{*}_{i},\pi^{*}_{-i};s^{\prime})+\epsilon_{i}\nu_{i}(\pi^{*}_{i};s^{\prime})]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\gamma\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot|s,\mathbf{a})}[\pi_{i}^{T}Q_{i}(s^{\prime},\cdot)p_{i}-\frac{1}{\tau_{i}}D_{i}(p_{i},\pi_{-i};s^{\prime})+\epsilon_{i}\nu_{i}(\pi_{i};s^{\prime})]

\displaystyle-\gamma\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot|s,\mathbf{a})}[(\pi_{i}^{*})^{T}Q_{i}(s^{\prime},\cdot)p_{i}-\frac{1}{\tau_{i}}D_{i}(p_{i},\pi^{*}_{-i};s^{\prime})+\epsilon_{i}\nu_{i}(\pi^{*}_{i};s^{\prime})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\gamma\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot|s,\mathbf{a})}[(\pi_{i}-\pi_{i}^{*})^{T}Q_{i}(s^{\prime},\cdot)p_{i}

\displaystyle-\frac{D_{i}(p_{i},\pi_{-i};s^{\prime})-D_{i}(p_{i},\pi^{*}_{-i};s^{\prime})}{\tau_{i}}+\epsilon_{i}(\nu_{i}(\pi_{i};s^{\prime})-\nu_{i}(\pi^{*}_{i};s^{\prime}))]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\gamma(\frac{\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{i}|}}{2}\textup{sp}(Q_{i})+\frac{L_{D}}{\tau_{i}}+\epsilon_{i}L_{\nu})\|z-z^{*}\|_{2}

taking max-norm on all possible
(s,\mathbf{a})
pairs completes the proof.
∎

Using
Section

G.2
, we obtain:

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathcal{T}_{z_{t}^{*}}\mathbf{Q}_{t}\|_{\max}

\displaystyle\leq\gamma(\frac{\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{\max}|}}{2}\textup{sp}(\mathbf{Q}_{t})+\frac{L_{D}}{\tau_{\min}}+\max\{\frac{L_{D}}{\tau_{\min}},\epsilon_{\max}L_{\nu}\})\|z_{t+1}-z_{t}^{*}\|_{2}

(128)

\displaystyle\leq\gamma(\frac{\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{\max}|}}{2}Q_{span}+\frac{L_{D}}{\tau_{\min}}+\max\{\frac{L_{D}}{\tau_{\min}},\epsilon_{\max}L_{\nu}\})\|z_{t+1}-z_{t}^{*}\|_{2}

Let
C_{\mathcal{T}}=\gamma(\frac{\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{\max}|}}{2}Q_{span}+\frac{L_{D}}{\tau_{\min}}+\max\{\frac{L_{D}}{\tau_{\min}},\epsilon_{\max}L_{\nu}\})
denote this coefficient, our iteration dynamics can be written as:

\displaystyle\|z_{t+1}-z_{t+1}^{*}\|_{2}

(129)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{1-2\beta_{t}\mu+\beta_{t}^{2}L_{F}^{2}}\|z_{t}-z_{t}^{*}\|_{2}+\alpha_{t}L_{RQE}\left(\|\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathcal{T}_{z_{t}^{*}}\mathbf{Q}_{t}\|_{\max}+(1+\gamma_{0})\|\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathbf{Q}^{*}\|_{\max}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{1-2\beta_{t}\mu+\beta_{t}^{2}L_{F}^{2}}\|z_{t}-z_{t}^{*}\|_{2}+\alpha_{t}L_{RQE}\left(C_{\mathcal{T}}\|z_{t+1}-z_{t}^{*}\|_{2}+(1+\gamma_{0})\|\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathbf{Q}^{*}\|_{\max}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{1-2\beta_{t}\mu+\beta_{t}^{2}L_{F}^{2}}\|z_{t}-z_{t}^{*}\|_{2}

\displaystyle+\alpha_{t}L_{RQE}\left(C_{\mathcal{T}}\sqrt{1-2\beta_{t}\mu+\beta_{t}^{2}L_{F}^{2}}\|z_{t}-z_{t}^{*}\|_{2}+(1+\gamma_{0})\|\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathbf{Q}^{*}\|_{\max}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1+\alpha_{t}L_{RQE}C_{\mathcal{T}})\sqrt{1-2\beta_{t}\mu+\beta_{t}^{2}L_{F}^{2}}\|z_{t}-z_{t}^{*}\|_{2}+(1+\gamma_{0})\alpha_{t}L_{RQE}\|\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathbf{Q}^{*}\|_{\max}.

where we have used (
121
) in the second inequality, and

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{Q}_{t+1}-\mathbf{Q}^{*}\|_{\max}\leq

\displaystyle\left(1-(1-\gamma_{0})\alpha_{t}\right)\|\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathbf{Q}^{*}\|_{\max}+\alpha_{t}\|\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathcal{T}_{z_{t}^{*}}\mathbf{Q}_{t}\|_{\max}

(130)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(1-(1-\gamma_{0})\alpha_{t}\right)\|\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathbf{Q}^{*}\|_{\max}+\alpha_{t}C_{\mathcal{T}}\|z_{t+1}-z_{t}^{*}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(1-(1-\gamma_{0})\alpha_{t}\right)\|\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathbf{Q}^{*}\|_{\max}+\alpha_{t}C_{\mathcal{T}}\sqrt{1-2\beta_{t}\mu+\beta_{t}^{2}L_{F}^{2}}\|z_{t}-z_{t}^{*}\|_{2}.

Notice that when
\beta_{t}\leq\min\{\frac{1}{\mu},\frac{\mu}{L_{F}^{2}}\}
, we have:

\sqrt{1-2\beta_{t}\mu+\beta_{t}^{2}L_{F}^{2}}\leq 1-\beta_{t}\mu+\frac{\beta_{t}^{2}L_{F}^{2}}{2}\leq 1-\frac{\beta_{t}}{2}\mu

(131)

Let
\tilde{\alpha}_{t}=(1-\gamma_{0})\alpha_{t}
and
\tilde{\beta}_{t}=\frac{\mu}{2}\beta_{t}
, our recursion becomes:

\displaystyle\|z_{t+1}-z_{t+1}^{*}\|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle(1+C_{1}\tilde{\alpha}_{t})(1-\tilde{\beta}_{t})\|z_{t}-z_{t}^{*}\|_{2}+C_{2}\tilde{\alpha}_{t}\|\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathbf{Q}^{*}\|_{\max}

(132)

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{Q}_{t+1}-\mathbf{Q}^{*}\|_{\max}\leq

\displaystyle(1-\tilde{\alpha}_{t})\|\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathbf{Q}^{*}\|_{\max}+C_{3}\tilde{\alpha}_{t}(1-\tilde{\beta}_{t})\|z_{t}-z_{t}^{*}\|_{2}

(133)

where
C_{1}=L_{RQE}C_{\mathcal{T}},C_{2}=\frac{1+\gamma_{0}}{1-\gamma_{0}}L_{RQE}
and
C_{3}=\frac{C_{\mathcal{T}}}{1-\gamma_{0}}
.
Let
v_{t}=\|\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathbf{Q}^{*}\|_{\max}
and
u_{t}=\|z_{t}-z_{t}^{*}\|_{2}
, we can rewrite the recursion in vector form:

\begin{bmatrix}u_{t+1}\\
v_{t+1}\end{bmatrix}\leq\begin{bmatrix}(1+C_{1}\tilde{\alpha}_{t})(1-\tilde{\beta}_{t})&C_{2}\tilde{\alpha}_{t}\\
C_{3}\tilde{\alpha}_{t}(1-\tilde{\beta}_{t})&1-\tilde{\alpha}_{t}\end{bmatrix}\cdot\begin{bmatrix}u_{t}\\
v_{t}\end{bmatrix}

(134)

we give different convergence results under different step size assumptions:

Scenario 1:
Assume we are using constant step sizes
\tilde{\alpha}_{t}=\tilde{\alpha},\tilde{\beta}_{t}=\tilde{\beta},\tilde{\alpha}\ll\tilde{\beta}
. The recursion becomes:

\begin{bmatrix}u_{t+1}\\
v_{t+1}\end{bmatrix}\leq\begin{bmatrix}(1+C_{1}\tilde{\alpha})(1-\tilde{\beta})&C_{2}\tilde{\alpha}\\
C_{3}\tilde{\alpha}(1-\tilde{\beta})&1-\tilde{\alpha}\end{bmatrix}\cdot\begin{bmatrix}u_{t}\\
v_{t}\end{bmatrix}

(135)

Let
W_{t}=u_{t}+kv_{t}
for some
k>0
, we have:

\displaystyle W_{t+1}=

\displaystyle u_{t+1}+kv_{t+1}

(136)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left((1+C_{1}\tilde{\alpha})(1-\tilde{\beta})+kC_{3}\tilde{\alpha}(1-\tilde{\beta})\right)u_{t}+\left(C_{2}\tilde{\alpha}+k(1-\tilde{\alpha})\right)v_{t}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max\left\{(1+C_{1}\tilde{\alpha})(1-\tilde{\beta})+kC_{3}\tilde{\alpha}(1-\tilde{\beta}),\frac{C_{2}\tilde{\alpha}}{k}+(1-\tilde{\alpha})\right\}(u_{t}+kv_{t})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max\left\{(1+(C_{1}+kC_{3})\tilde{\alpha})(1-\tilde{\beta}),\frac{C_{2}\tilde{\alpha}}{k}+(1-\tilde{\alpha})\right\}W_{t}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max\left\{1-\tilde{\beta}+(C_{1}+kC_{3})\tilde{\alpha},1-(1-\frac{C_{2}}{k})\tilde{\alpha}\right\}W_{t}

take
k=2C_{2}
and
\tilde{\beta}\geq\left(\frac{1}{2}+C_{1}+2C_{2}C_{3}\right)\tilde{\alpha}
, we have:

W_{t}\leq\left(1-\frac{\tilde{\alpha}}{2}\right)^{t}W_{0}\leq\left(1-\frac{\tilde{\alpha}}{2}\right)^{t}\left(2\left(\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{1}|}+\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{2}|}\right)+k\|\mathbf{Q}^{*}\|_{\max}\right),

(137)

and consequently,

\displaystyle\|z_{t}-z_{t}^{*}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq W_{t}\leq 2\left(1-\frac{\tilde{\alpha}}{2}\right)^{t}\left(\left(\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{1}|}+\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{2}|}\right)+C_{2}Q_{\max}\right);

(138)

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathbf{Q}^{*}\|_{\max}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{W_{t}}{k}\leq\left(1-\frac{\tilde{\alpha}}{2}\right)^{t}\left(\frac{1}{C_{2}}\left(\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{1}|}+\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{2}|}\right)+Q_{\max}\right).

(139)

Let
z^{*}
be the RQE of the Markov game, we know that
z^{*}
is also the state-wise RQE of
\mathbf{Q}^{*}
.
Theorem

3.1
implies:

\|z_{t}^{*}-z^{*}\|_{2}\leq L_{RQE}\|\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathbf{Q}^{*}\|_{\max},

(140)

so that

\displaystyle\|z_{t}-z^{*}\|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\|z_{t}-z_{t}^{*}\|_{2}+\|z_{t}^{*}-z^{*}\|_{2}

(141)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{\tilde{\alpha}}{2}\right)^{t}\left(\left(\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{1}|}+\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{2}|}\right)+C_{2}Q_{\max}\right)

\displaystyle+L_{RQE}\left(1-\frac{\tilde{\alpha}}{2}\right)^{t}\left(\frac{1}{C_{2}}\left(\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{1}|}+\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{2}|}\right)+\|\mathbf{Q}^{*}\|_{\max}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{\tilde{\alpha}}{2}\right)^{t}\left(1+\frac{L_{RQE}}{C_{2}}\right)\left(\left(\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{1}|}+\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{2}|}\right)+C_{2}Q_{\max}\right).

Scenario 2:
Assume we are using diminishing step sizes where
\tilde{\alpha}_{t}=\frac{\tilde{\alpha}}{t+h}
and
\tilde{\beta}_{t}=\frac{\tilde{\beta}}{t+h},\tilde{\alpha}\ll\tilde{\beta}
for some fixed integer
h
. The recursion now has the form:

\begin{bmatrix}u_{t+1}\\
v_{t+1}\end{bmatrix}\leq\begin{bmatrix}(1+C_{1}\frac{\tilde{\alpha}}{t+h})(1-\frac{\tilde{\beta}}{t+h})&C_{2}\frac{\tilde{\alpha}}{t+h}\\
C_{3}\frac{\tilde{\alpha}}{t+h}(1-\frac{\tilde{\beta}}{t+h})&1-\frac{\tilde{\alpha}}{t+h}\end{bmatrix}\cdot\begin{bmatrix}u_{t}\\
v_{t}\end{bmatrix}.

(142)

Similarly, let
W_{t}=u_{t}+kv_{t}
for some
k>0
, the recursion on
W_{t}
becomes:

\displaystyle W_{t+1}=

\displaystyle u_{t+1}+kv_{t+1}

(143)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left((1+C_{1}\frac{\tilde{\alpha}}{t+h})(1-\frac{\tilde{\beta}}{t+h})+kC_{3}\frac{\tilde{\alpha}}{t+h}(1-\frac{\tilde{\beta}}{t+h})\right)u_{t}+\left(C_{2}\frac{\tilde{\alpha}}{t+h}+k(1-\frac{\tilde{\alpha}}{t+h})\right)v_{t}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max\left\{(1+C_{1}\frac{\tilde{\alpha}}{t+h})(1-\frac{\tilde{\beta}}{t+h})+kC_{3}\frac{\tilde{\alpha}}{t+h}(1-\frac{\tilde{\beta}}{t+h}),\frac{C_{2}}{k}\frac{\tilde{\alpha}}{t+h}+(1-\frac{\tilde{\alpha}}{t+h})\right\}(u_{t}+kv_{t})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max\left\{(1+(C_{1}+kC_{3})\frac{\tilde{\alpha}}{t+h})(1-\frac{\tilde{\beta}}{t+h}),\frac{C_{2}}{k}\frac{\tilde{\alpha}}{t+h}+(1-\frac{\tilde{\alpha}}{t+h})\right\}W_{t}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max\left\{1-\frac{\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{\alpha}(C_{1}+kC_{3})}{t+h},1-\frac{(1-C_{2}/k)\tilde{\alpha}}{t+h}\right\}W_{t}.

Setting
k=2C_{2}
and
\tilde{\beta}\geq\left(\frac{1}{2}+C_{1}+2C_{2}C_{3}\right)\tilde{\alpha}
, we have:

W_{t+1}\leq\left(1-\frac{\tilde{\alpha}}{2(t+h)}\right)W_{t}

(144)

and as a result,

\displaystyle W_{t+1}\leq

\displaystyle W_{0}\prod_{i=0}^{t}\left(1-\frac{\tilde{\alpha}}{2(i+h)}\right)

(145)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle W_{0}\prod_{i=0}^{t}\exp\left(-\frac{\tilde{\alpha}}{2(i+h)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle W_{0}\exp\left(-\frac{\tilde{\alpha}}{2}\sum_{i=0}^{t}\frac{1}{i+h}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle W_{0}\exp\left(-\frac{\tilde{\alpha}}{2}(H_{h+t}-H_{h-1})\right).

where
H_{t}
denotes the harmonic series. Since we have the following upper bound:

H_{h-1}-H_{h+t}\leq\log\frac{h}{h+t+1}

(146)

we obtain the upper bound of:

W_{t+1}\leq W_{0}\left(\frac{h}{h+t+1}\right)^{\frac{\tilde{\alpha}}{2}}

(147)

which leads to the final result:

\displaystyle\|z_{t}-z_{t}^{*}\|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle 2\left(\left(\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{1}|}+\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{2}|}\right)+C_{2}Q_{\max}\right)\left(\frac{h}{h+t+1}\right)^{\frac{\tilde{\alpha}}{2}};

(148)

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathbf{Q}^{*}\|_{\max}\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{C_{2}}\left(\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{1}|}+\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{2}|}\right)+Q_{\max}\right)\left(\frac{h}{h+t+1}\right)^{\frac{\tilde{\alpha}}{2}}.

(149)

and:

\|z_{t}-z^{*}\|_{2}\leq\left(1+\frac{L_{RQE}}{C_{2}}\right)\left(\left(\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{1}|}+\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{2}|}\right)+C_{2}Q_{\max}\right)\left(\frac{h}{h+t+1}\right)^{\frac{\tilde{\alpha}}{2}}.

(150)

∎

Appendix H
Detailed Statement and Proof of
Theorem

4.7

We first present a more detailed statement of
Theorem

4.7
as follows:

Theorem H.1

(
Theorem

4.7
, detailed)
.

Under Assumptions
4.3
and
4.3
, if there exists
\gamma_{0}
such that the Bellman optimality operator
\mathcal{T}
is a contraction mapping, and additionally
D_{i}(\cdot,\cdot)
are
L_{D}
-Lipschitz in either argument when the other is fixed,
\nu_{i}(\cdot)
are
L_{\nu}
-Lipschitz with respect to its input, and the gradient operator of the modified 4-player game is
(\mu,\lambda)
-strongly monotone and
L_{F}
-Lipschitz for every state
s\in\mathcal{S}
, then both on- and off-policy variants of
Algorithm

1
with the following parameters:

\displaystyle K\geq

\displaystyle\frac{4\sqrt{2}C}{(1-\rho)(1-\gamma_{0})d_{\min}};

(151)

\displaystyle\alpha_{t}<

\displaystyle 1;

(152)

\displaystyle\beta_{t}\leq

\displaystyle\min\{\frac{1}{\mu},\frac{\mu}{L_{F}^{2}}\};

(153)

\displaystyle\frac{\beta_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}\geq

\displaystyle\frac{(1-\gamma_{0})d_{\min{}}/2+\left(1+(1+\gamma_{0})^{2}+2C_{\mathcal{T}}+\frac{8\gamma^{2}C_{\mathcal{T}}^{2}}{d_{\min{}}^{3}}\left(\frac{1+\gamma_{0}}{1-\gamma_{0}}\right)^{2}\right)L_{RQE}}{\mu+\left(1+(1+\gamma_{0})^{2}+2C_{\mathcal{T}}+\frac{8\gamma^{2}C_{\mathcal{T}}^{2}}{d_{\min{}}^{3}}\left(\frac{1+\gamma_{0}}{1-\gamma_{0}}\right)^{2}\right)L_{RQE}}.

(154)

then in expectation, the sequence of
z_{t}
converges to the RQE
z^{*}
of the Markov game and the
Q
function
\mathbf{Q}_{t}
converges to the corresponding
\mathbf{Q}^{*}
to
z^{*}
at the following rates:

1.

If we use constant step sizes
\alpha_{t}=\alpha,\beta_{t}=\beta
, then
\forall s\in\mathcal{S}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathbf{Q}^{*}\|_{\max}^{2}]\leq(1-\frac{1}{2}(1-\gamma_{0})d_{\min{}}\alpha)^{t}(Q_{\max}^{2}+8k)+\frac{2(C_{3}+kC_{6})}{(1-\gamma_{0})d_{\min{}}}\alpha;

(155)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|z_{t}(\cdot|s)-z^{*}(\cdot|s)\|_{2}^{2}]\leq(2+2kL_{RQE}^{2})\left((1-\frac{1}{2}(1-\gamma_{0})d_{\min{}}\alpha)^{t}(Q_{\max}^{2}/k+8)+\frac{2(C_{3}/k+C_{6})}{(1-\gamma_{0})d_{\min{}}}\alpha\right).

2.

If we use diminishing step sizes
\alpha_{t}=\frac{\alpha}{t+h},\beta_{t}=\frac{\beta}{t+h}
for some
h\geq 1
, then
\forall s\in\mathcal{S}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathbf{Q}^{*}\|_{\max}^{2}]\leq\left(Q_{\max}^{2}+8k+\frac{(C_{3}+kC_{6})\alpha^{2}}{h^{2}}\frac{h+2-\frac{1}{2}(1-\gamma_{0})d_{\min{}}\alpha}{1-\frac{1}{2}(1-\gamma_{0})d_{\min{}}\alpha}\right)\left(\frac{h+1}{h+t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}(1-\gamma_{0})d_{\min{}}\alpha};

(156)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|z_{t}(\cdot|s)-z^{*}(\cdot|s)\|_{2}^{2}]\leq(2/k+2L_{RQE}^{2})\left(Q_{\max}^{2}+8k+\frac{(C_{3}+kC_{6})\alpha^{2}}{h^{2}}\frac{h+2-\frac{1}{2}(1-\gamma_{0})d_{\min{}}\alpha}{1-\frac{1}{2}(1-\gamma_{0})d_{\min{}}\alpha}\right)\left(\frac{h+1}{h+t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}(1-\gamma_{0})d_{\min{}}\alpha}.

where in both cases,

\displaystyle d_{\min}=

\displaystyle\underline{\mu}\underline{\pi};

\displaystyle k=

\displaystyle\frac{(1-\gamma_{0})d_{\min{}}}{4L_{RQE}(1+\gamma_{0})^{2}};

\displaystyle C_{3}=

\displaystyle 4(1-\gamma_{0})^{2}\left(\frac{1}{K}+\frac{64C\rho}{(1-\rho)K}+2\left(\frac{C}{(1-\rho)K}\right)^{2}\right)Q_{\max}^{2}

\displaystyle+\left(1+\frac{1}{2}(1-\gamma_{0})d_{\min{}}\alpha+\frac{1}{K}+\frac{64C\rho}{(1-\rho)K}+2\left(\frac{C}{(1-\rho)K}\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\times 8\gamma^{2}C_{\mathcal{T}}^{2}(1-\beta)^{2}+\frac{8}{K}Q_{span}^{2};

\displaystyle C_{6}=

\displaystyle(2+C_{\mathcal{T}})L_{RQE}^{2}C_{\mathcal{T}}(1-\beta)^{2}+L_{RQE}^{3}C_{\mathcal{T}}^{2}\alpha_{t}(1-\beta)^{2}+4L_{RQE}^{2}(1+\gamma_{0})^{2}Q_{\max{}}

here
Q_{\max},Q_{span}
are those provided in
Section

G.2
, and
L_{RQE},C_{\mathcal{T}}
are the same as those in
Theorem

G.2
.

Proof of
Theorem

H.1
:

In this section, we provide a proof of
Theorem

H.1
following similar ideas as that of
Theorem

G.2
. However, since the iterates of
Algorithm

1
incurs coupled random updates, we use a coupled Lyapunov drift approach following prior work in stochastic approximation that considers the Lyapunov function of
\|\mathbf{Q}-\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\|_{\max}^{2}
as opposed to
\|\mathbf{Q}-\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\|_{\max}
in the proof of
Theorem

G.2
. We present the coupled Lyapunov drift inequalities respectively in this section.

H.1
Lyapunov Drift Inequality for
Q
Functions

Before presenting the Lyapunov drift inequality, we first prove a lemma that will be useful throughout:

Lemma H.2
.

Assume the Bellman optimality operator satisfy
\gamma_{0}
-contraction property, the following bound holds for all
t
and arbitrary constant
c>0
:

\|\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathbf{Q}_{t}\|_{\max}^{2}\leq(1+c)\|\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathcal{T}_{z_{t}^{*}}\mathbf{Q}_{t}\|^{2}_{\max}+\left(1+\frac{1}{c}\right)(1-\gamma_{0})^{2}\|\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathbf{Q}^{*}\|^{2}_{\max}.

(157)

Proof.

We can write the difference term as:

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathbf{Q}_{t}\|_{\max}

(158)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathcal{T}_{z_{t}^{*}}\mathbf{Q}_{t}+\mathcal{T}_{z_{t}^{*}}\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathbf{Q}^{*}-\mathbf{Q}_{t}+\mathbf{Q}^{*}\|_{\max}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathcal{T}_{z_{t}^{*}}\mathbf{Q}_{t}\|_{\max}+\|\mathcal{T}_{z_{t}^{*}}\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathcal{T}\mathbf{Q}^{*}-\mathbf{Q}_{t}+\mathbf{Q}^{*}\|_{\max}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathcal{T}_{z_{t}^{*}}\mathbf{Q}_{t}\|_{\max}+(1-\gamma_{0})\|\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathbf{Q}^{*}\|_{\max}

therefore, we can bound the square term as:

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathbf{Q}_{t}\|_{\max}^{2}

(159)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\|\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathcal{T}_{z_{t}^{*}}\mathbf{Q}_{t}\|_{\max}+(1-\gamma_{0})\|\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathbf{Q}^{*}\|_{\max}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(1+c)\|\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathcal{T}_{z_{t}^{*}}\mathbf{Q}_{t}\|^{2}_{\max}+\left(1+\frac{1}{c}\right)(1-\gamma_{0})^{2}\|\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathbf{Q}^{*}\|^{2}_{\max}

where we have used
Appendix

I
in the last inequality.
∎

Let
\mathcal{F}_{t,k}
denote the filtration at timestep
k
in episode
t
and
\mathcal{F}_{t}
denote
\mathcal{F}_{t,0}
, for
w_{i,k}=\hat{q}_{i,k}-\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}Q_{i,t}(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})
, we have that
\mathbb{E}[w_{i,k}|\mathcal{F}_{t,k}]=0
. Therefore, let
\xi_{i,k}(s,\mathbf{a})
denote
\xi_{i,k}(s,\mathbf{a})=w_{i,k}\mathbf{1}[(s,\mathbf{a})=(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})]
, for the conditional expectation given
\mathcal{F}_{t}
, it holds that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\xi_{i,k}(s,\mathbf{a})|\mathcal{F}_{t,k}]=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[w_{i,k}\mathbf{1}[(s,\mathbf{a})=(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})]|\mathcal{F}_{t,k}]

(160)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[w_{i,k}|\mathcal{F}_{t,k}]\mathbf{1}[(s,\mathbf{a})=(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\xi_{i,k}(s,\mathbf{a})|\mathcal{F}_{t}]=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[w_{i,k}\mathbf{1}[(s,\mathbf{a})=(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})]|\mathcal{F}_{t}]

(161)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[w_{i,k}\mathbf{1}[(s,\mathbf{a})=(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})]|\mathcal{F}_{t,k}]|\mathcal{F}_{t}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[w_{i,k}|\mathcal{F}_{t,k}]\mathbf{1}[(s,\mathbf{a})=(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})]|\mathcal{F}_{t}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0

therefore,
\xi_{i,k}(s,\mathbf{a})
is a martingale difference sequence with respect to
\{\mathcal{F}_{t,k}\}
. Let
M_{i,t}(s,\mathbf{a})=\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\xi_{i,k}(s,\mathbf{a})
, we can see that
\mathbb{E}[M_{i,t}(s,\mathbf{a})|\mathcal{F}_{t}]=0
. For the update
\hat{\delta}_{i}
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\hat{\delta}_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})|\mathcal{F}_{t}]

(162)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\hat{q}_{i,k}-Q_{i,t}(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})\right)\mathbf{1}[(s,\mathbf{a})=(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})]\middle|\mathcal{F}_{t}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(w_{i,k}+\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}Q_{i,t}(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})-Q_{i,t}(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})\right)\mathbf{1}[(s,\mathbf{a})=(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})]\middle|\mathcal{F}_{t}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}Q_{i,t}(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})-Q_{i,t}(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})\right)\mathbf{1}[(s,\mathbf{a})=(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})]\middle|\mathcal{F}_{t}\right]+\mathbb{E}[M_{i,t}(s,\mathbf{a})|\mathcal{F}_{t}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}Q_{i,t}(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})-Q_{i,t}(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})\right)\mathbf{1}[(s,\mathbf{a})=(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})]\middle|\mathcal{F}_{t}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}Q_{i,t}(s,\mathbf{a})-Q_{i,t}(s,\mathbf{a})\right)\mathbf{1}[(s,\mathbf{a})=(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})]\middle|\mathcal{F}_{t}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}Q_{i,t}(s,\mathbf{a})-Q_{i,t}(s,\mathbf{a})\right)\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\Pr((s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})=(s,\mathbf{a})|\mathcal{F}_{t})

let
\bar{P}_{K}(s,\mathbf{a}|\mathcal{F}_{t})
denote
\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\Pr((s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})=(s,\mathbf{a})|\mathcal{F}_{t})
, we conclude that

\mathbb{E}[\hat{\delta}_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})|\mathcal{F}_{t}]=\bar{P}_{K}(s,\mathbf{a}|\mathcal{F}_{t})\left(\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}Q_{i,t}(s,\mathbf{a})-Q_{i,t}(s,\mathbf{a})\right)

(163)

Critic Update Decomposition.

We first provide a decomposition result for
(Q_{i,t+1}(s,\mathbf{a})-Q_{i}^{*}(s,\mathbf{a}))^{2}
, notice that the update rule of
Algorithm

1
can be rewritten as:

\displaystyle Q_{i,t+1}(s,\mathbf{a})=

\displaystyle Q_{i,t}(s,\mathbf{a})+\alpha_{t}\hat{\delta}_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})

(164)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle Q_{i,t}(s,\mathbf{a})+\alpha_{t}\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\left(\hat{q}_{i,k}-Q_{i,t}(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})\right)\mathbf{1}[(s,\mathbf{a})=(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle Q_{i,t}(s,\mathbf{a})+\alpha_{t}\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\left(w_{i,k}+\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}Q_{i,t}(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})-Q_{i,t}(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})\right)\mathbf{1}[(s,\mathbf{a})=(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle Q_{i,t}(s,\mathbf{a})+\alpha_{t}\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\xi_{i,k}(s,\mathbf{a})

\displaystyle+\alpha_{t}\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\left(\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}Q_{i,t}(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})-Q_{i,t}(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})\right)\mathbf{1}[(s,\mathbf{a})=(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle Q_{i,t}(s,\mathbf{a})+\alpha_{t}\mathbb{E}[\hat{\delta}_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})|\mathcal{F}_{t}]

\displaystyle+\alpha_{t}\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\left(\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}Q_{i,t}(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})-Q_{i,t}(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})\right)\mathbf{1}[(s,\mathbf{a})=(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})]-\alpha_{t}\mathbb{E}[\hat{\delta}_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})|\mathcal{F}_{t}]

\displaystyle+\alpha_{t}\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\xi_{i,k}(s,\mathbf{a})

since we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(Q_{i,t}(s,\mathbf{a})+\alpha_{t}\mathbb{E}[\hat{\delta}_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})|\mathcal{F}_{t}]-Q^{*}_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})\right)\left(\alpha_{t}\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\xi_{i,k}(s,\mathbf{a})\right)\middle|\mathcal{F}_{t}\right]

(165)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(Q_{i,t}(s,\mathbf{a})+\alpha_{t}\mathbb{E}[\hat{\delta}_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})|\mathcal{F}_{t}]-Q^{*}_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\alpha_{t}\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\xi_{i,k}(s,\mathbf{a})\right)\middle|\mathcal{F}_{t}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(Q_{i,t}(s,\mathbf{a})+\alpha_{t}\mathbb{E}[\hat{\delta}_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})|\mathcal{F}_{t}]-Q^{*}_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})\right)\alpha_{t}\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\xi_{i,k}(s,\mathbf{a})\middle|\mathcal{F}_{t}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0;

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\left(Q_{i,t}(s,\mathbf{a})+\alpha_{t}\mathbb{E}[\hat{\delta}_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})|\mathcal{F}_{t}]-Q^{*}_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})\right)\times

(166)

\displaystyle\left(\alpha_{t}\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\left(\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}Q_{i,t}(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})-Q_{i,t}(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})\right)\mathbf{1}[(s,\mathbf{a})=(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})]-\alpha_{t}\mathbb{E}[\hat{\delta}_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})|\mathcal{F}_{t}]\right)\Bigg|\mathcal{F}_{t}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(Q_{i,t}(s,\mathbf{a})+\alpha_{t}\mathbb{E}[\hat{\delta}_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})|\mathcal{F}_{t}]-Q^{*}_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})\right)\times

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\alpha_{t}\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\left(\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}Q_{i,t}(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})-Q_{i,t}(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})\right)\mathbf{1}[(s,\mathbf{a})=(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})]-\alpha_{t}\mathbb{E}[\hat{\delta}_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})|\mathcal{F}_{t}]\middle|\mathcal{F}_{t}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(Q_{i,t}(s,\mathbf{a})+\alpha_{t}\mathbb{E}[\hat{\delta}_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})|\mathcal{F}_{t}]-Q^{*}_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})\right)\times

\displaystyle\left(\alpha_{t}\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}Q_{i,t}(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})-Q_{i,t}(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})\right)\mathbf{1}[(s,\mathbf{a})=(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})]\middle|\mathcal{F}_{t}\right]-\alpha_{t}\mathbb{E}[\hat{\delta}_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})|\mathcal{F}_{t}]\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0.

We can rewrite the squared difference term
(Q_{i,t+1}(s,\mathbf{a})-Q_{i}^{*}(s,\mathbf{a}))^{2}
into the sum of 4 different terms:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(Q_{i,t+1}(s,\mathbf{a})-Q^{*}_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})\right)^{2}\middle|\mathcal{F}_{t}\right]

(167)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[\left(Q_{i,t}(s,\mathbf{a})+\alpha_{t}\mathbb{E}[\hat{\delta}_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})|\mathcal{F}_{t}]-Q^{*}_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})\right)^{2}\middle|\mathcal{F}_{t}\right]}_{\mathcal{L}_{1}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\alpha_{t}\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\left(\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}Q_{i,t}(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})-Q_{i,t}(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})\right)\mathbf{1}[(s,\mathbf{a})=(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})]-\alpha_{t}\mathbb{E}[\hat{\delta}_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})|\mathcal{F}_{t}]\right)^{2}\middle|\mathcal{F}_{t}\right]}_{\mathcal{L}_{2}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\alpha_{t}\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\xi_{i,k}(s,\mathbf{a})\right)^{2}\middle|\mathcal{F}_{t}\right]}_{\mathcal{L}_{3}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle+2\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\left(\alpha_{t}\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\xi_{i,k}(s,\mathbf{a})\right)\times

\displaystyle\underbrace{\left(\alpha_{t}\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\left(\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}Q_{i,t}(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})-Q_{i,t}(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})\right)\mathbf{1}[(s,\mathbf{a})=(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})]-\alpha_{t}\mathbb{E}[\hat{\delta}_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})|\mathcal{F}_{t}]\right)\Bigg|\mathcal{F}_{t}\Bigg]}_{\mathcal{L}_{4}^{\prime}}

Here
\mathcal{L}_{1}^{\prime}
indicates the expected update of
Q_{i}
conditioned on
\mathcal{F}_{t}
,
\mathcal{L}_{2}^{\prime},\mathcal{L}_{3}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{L}_{4}^{\prime}
are error terms induced by the stochasticity induced by sampling in the update rule of
Algorithm

1
. We can take max-norm on both sides of (
167
) and get:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathbf{Q}_{t+1}-\mathbf{Q}^{*}\|_{\max}^{2}\middle|\mathcal{F}_{t}\right]

(168)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mathbf{Q}_{t}+\alpha_{t}\mathbb{E}[\hat{\boldsymbol{\delta}}|\mathcal{F}_{t}]-\mathbf{Q}^{*}\right\|_{\max}^{2}\middle|\mathcal{F}_{t}\right]}_{\mathcal{L}_{1}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{t}\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\left(\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}\mathbf{Q}_{t}(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})-\mathbf{Q}_{t}(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})\right)\otimes e_{(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})}-\alpha_{t}\mathbb{E}[\hat{\boldsymbol{\delta}}|\mathcal{F}_{t}]\right\|_{\max}^{2}\middle|\mathcal{F}_{t}\right]}_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{t}\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{k}\right\|_{\max}^{2}\middle|\mathcal{F}_{t}\right]}_{\mathcal{L}_{3}}

\displaystyle+2\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{t}\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\left(\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}\mathbf{Q}_{t}(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})-\mathbf{Q}_{t}(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})\right)\otimes e_{(s_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})}-\alpha_{t}\mathbb{E}[\hat{\boldsymbol{\delta}}|\mathcal{F}_{t}]\right\|_{\max}\times\left\|\alpha_{t}\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{k}\right\|_{\max}\middle|\mathcal{F}_{t}\right]}_{\mathcal{L}_{4}}

To obtain a Lyapunov drift inequality on
\|\mathbf{Q}_{t}-\mathbf{Q}^{*}\|_{\max}^{2}
, we only have to bound
\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\mathcal{L}_{3}
and
\mathcal{L}_{4}
, and each
\mathcal{L}_{i}
term can be bounded individually, as we will show as follows.

Further decomposing the expected update term
\mathcal{L}_{1}
.

Although the update expressed in term
\mathcal{L}_{1}
is no longer stochastic conditioned on
\mathcal{F}_{t}
, it is not directly tied to the Bellman operator
\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}
and therefore cannot directly be processed by a contraction argument. Therefore, we have to further decompose
\mathcal{L}_{1}
into terms that we can directly bound. To accommodate a cleaner representation, notice that

\displaystyle Q_{i,t}(s,\mathbf{a})+\alpha_{t}\mathbb{E}[\hat{\delta}_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})|\mathcal{F}_{t}]-Q^{*}_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})

(169)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle Q_{i,t}(s,\mathbf{a})+\alpha_{t}d_{t+1}(s,\mathbf{a})\left(\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}Q_{i,t}(s,\mathbf{a})-Q_{i,t}(s,\mathbf{a})\right)-Q^{*}_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})

\displaystyle+\alpha_{t}\mathbb{E}[\hat{\delta}_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})|\mathcal{F}_{t}]-\alpha_{t}d_{t+1}(s,\mathbf{a})\left(\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}Q_{i,t}(s,\mathbf{a})-Q_{i,t}(s,\mathbf{a})\right)

we have the following decomposition for
\mathcal{L}_{1}^{\prime}
:

\mathcal{L}_{1}^{\prime}=\mathcal{L}_{1,1}^{\prime}+2\alpha_{t}\mathcal{L}_{1,2}^{\prime}+\alpha_{t}^{2}\mathcal{L}_{1,3}^{\prime}

(170)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{1,1}^{\prime}=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(Q_{i,t}(s,\mathbf{a})+\alpha_{t}d_{t+1}(s,\mathbf{a})\left(\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}Q_{i,t}(s,\mathbf{a})-Q_{i,t}(s,\mathbf{a})\right)-Q^{*}_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})\right)^{2}\middle|\mathcal{F}_{t}\right];

(171)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{1,2}^{\prime}=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\left(Q_{i,t}(s,\mathbf{a})+\alpha_{t}d_{t+1}(s,\mathbf{a})\left(\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}Q_{i,t}(s,\mathbf{a})-Q_{i,t}(s,\mathbf{a})\right)-Q^{*}_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})\right)

\displaystyle\quad\times\left(\mathbb{E}[\hat{\delta}_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})|\mathcal{F}_{t}]-d_{t+1}(s,\mathbf{a})\left(\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}Q_{i,t}(s,\mathbf{a})-Q_{i,t}(s,\mathbf{a})\right)\right)\Big|\mathcal{F}_{t}\Big];

(172)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{1,3}^{\prime}=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\mathbb{E}[\hat{\delta}_{i}(s,\mathbf{a})|\mathcal{F}_{t}]-d_{t+1}(s,\mathbf{a})\left(\mathcal{T}_{z_{t+1}}Q_{i,t}(s,\mathbf{a})-Q_{i,t}(s,\mathbf{a})\right)\right)^{2}\middle|\mathcal{F}_{t}\right].

(173)

here
d_{t+1}
is the steady state-action distribution induced by the sampling policy at time step
t+1
: For on-policy updates,

d_{t+1}(s,\mathbf{a})=\mu_{\pi_{t+1}}(s)\pi_{t+1}(\mathbf{a}|s).

(174)

and for off-policy updates,

d_{t+1}(s,\mathbf{a})=\mu_{\pi^{r}}(s)\pi^{r}(\mathbf{a}|s).

(175)

for the corresponding state distribution
\mu_{\pi_{t+1}}
or
\mu_{\pi^{r}}
. Taking max-norm of each term above, we obtain the following bound:

\mathcal{L}_{1}\leq\mathcal{L}_{1,1}+2\alpha_{t}\mathcal{L}_{1,2}+\alpha_{t}^{2}\mathcal{L}_{1,3}

(176)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{1,1}=
